## 2026-07-24 â€” Phase 8 "feel" layer: Auroral Ring, Heliosphere Pulse, diegetic text, Â§11 opening sequence (Claude Code)

Decisions made while building the ambient/feel layer of `/explore`. All four workstreams are additive to the scene Antigravity's Sky Anchor + camera rig landed; none touch the ISS/planet click logic (Antigravity's parallel task).

- **Â§7.1's open "Ambient: CSS vs GSAP" question (flagged in `motion.ts`) â€” answered: GSAP.** The ambient effects Phase 8 actually needed (aurora curtain sway, heliosphere wash) animate **WebGL shader uniforms**, which CSS cannot reach. Both loop via `gsap.to(..., { ease: 'sine.inOut', repeat: -1 })` inside Â§7.1's 4â€“20s band (sway 14s, wash 4â€“20s â€” see below), so the easing vocabulary stays GSAP's everywhere. The CSS `--ease-sine-in-out` custom property remains valid for any future DOM-side ambient effect. `motion.ts`'s open-question comment left for the next reader is now answered here.
- **Live data path: SSE `/stream`, not `/api/brief` polling.** The Auroral Ring (Kp) and Heliosphere Pulse (solar wind speed) consume the fast-tier stream â€” it exists precisely to carry live fast-tier data (`ARCHITECTURE.md` Â§4), pushes `kpCurrent.estimatedKp` (Â§7's documented primary Kp) and `rtswPlasma.protonSpeed` (API_SOURCES.md's fast-tier section names Heliosphere Pulse as a consumer). One `EventSource` in `ExplorePage` (`useSpaceWeather`) feeds both objects. Added `/stream` to the Vite dev proxy (it lives beside `/api`, same backend). The source's own `fetchedAt`/`healthy` pass through untouched; `streamedAt` is deliberately not exposed (freshness honesty, Â§7.3).
- **Auroral Ring in the ground view = glow band toward the geomagnetic pole.** Â§11's "Earth wrapped in the live Auroral Ring" describes the orbit view, which doesn't exist yet; from the ground, the same live phenomenon is a horizon glow toward the observer's geomagnetic pole. Everything physical comes from the shared Â§7 engine: `auroraVisibility()` gates rendering, Â§11's `auroraStrengthToFactor()` drives how wide/high the band reaches (0 = sliver on the horizon, 1 = toward zenith). `kp === null` (source down) renders nothing per API_SOURCES.md's SWPC fallback â€” no invented baseline glow. The only new math is the great-circle initial bearing to the pole (rendering placement, not prediction â€” kept in the component, not `packages/shared`). Color is the `--color-aurora` token read at runtime via a new `cssColorToken()` helper (WebGL can't consume CSS vars; hardcoding hex would repeat the drift Phase 7's cleanup removed).
- **Heliosphere Pulse = radial wash from the Sun's true direction; wind speed â†’ tempo.** Sun direction from the shared Â§4 engine (`sunHorizontalPosition` â€” real geometry, below the horizon at night). Live RTSW proton speed maps linearly from 250â€“800 km/s onto a 20â†’4s wash period (clamped), i.e. faster wind literally pulses faster, always inside Â§7.1's ambient band. The mapping constants are invented presentation (no doc pins them) â€” flagged here for review. Speed changes retime the running tween via `timeScale` (no mid-wash restart jump). No data â†’ no pulse; unhealthy-but-present data renders at 50% (mirrors Â§10's dimmed-unavailable convention). Brass-500 token, additive, peak alpha â‰ˆ 0.1 â€” instrument-quiet by design.
- **Diegetic text: troika directly (no drei), self-hosted Martian Mono TTF.** `troika-three-text` is the locked library (`ARCHITECTURE.md` Â§2); drei's `<Text>` is just a wrapper and drei isn't installed â€” using troika directly avoids a large transitive dependency for one component. Troika 0.52.4 ships no TS types and none exist on DefinitelyTyped â†’ local `types/troika-three-text.d.ts` declaring exactly the surface used. Troika parses font files itself (no woff2), so the DOM's Google-Fonts pipeline can't serve it: added `public/fonts/MartianMono-Regular.ttf` (static, 84KB, same face the DOM loads) + its OFL license file. The Â§11 screen-reader DOM mirror is a small dedicated zustand registry (`diegeticTextStore`) + an `sr-only aria-live="polite"` mirror component outside the Canvas â€” every `DiegeticText` registers/updates/unregisters automatically, so the mirror can never drift from the scene by construction.
- **Cardinal marks (N/E/S/W) added as the first diegetic consumer â€” flagged as a deviation-adjacent addition.** Â§11 doesn't name compass marks; they're instrument furniture in the same class as the SkyDome's horizon hairline (mono, 55% opacity, non-clickable, zero Rule-of-7 slots), they make the ground view orientable, and they prove the troika+mirror system live. If the human wants them gone, deleting `CardinalMarks.tsx` is one line in `ExplorePage`.
- **Opening sequence: DOM veil, not a second render path.** The scene renders from frame one under a black veil; "the sky fades up" (0:04) is the veil lifting at cinematic `expo.inOut` (1.6s â€” Â§11 states no duration for this row; used the cinematic upper bound). First-visit-only via `localStorage` (`astranet:explore:visited`), set on sequence completion so an interrupted first load replays. Â§11's full table (0:12 ISS, 0:20 click-rise **1.6s expo.inOut â€” stated, encoded verbatim**, 0:26 tether text, 0:40 lock break) lives in `motion.ts`'s `OPENING_SEQUENCE` so Antigravity's ISS half runs off the same table, not a re-typed copy. Â§7.6 honored: under `prefers-reduced-motion` the sequence collapses to a single 200ms fade, ambient loops never start, and intensity changes use the 200ms fade. Ambient audio (Â§11's wind bed) is **not built** â€” no audio asset or pipeline exists anywhere in the repo; deferred, noted in PROGRESS.md.
- **Nav auto-hide (Â§11):** first camera-motion event (drag/wheel) starts one 3s timer â†’ hide; pointer within 24px of the top edge â†’ show. "3 seconds of camera motion" is read as "3s after motion begins," the simplest predictable reading; revisit if the human intended cumulative motion time.

## 2026-07-24 â€” Phase 8 clickable-markers verification: NEO marker removed from /explore, ISS pass logic consolidated, Jupiter color logged (Claude Code)

Independent verification of Antigravity's "ISS + planet markers as clickable objects" task found two real defects (fixed) and one undocumented color choice (logged, accepted).

- **NEO marker removed from the 3D scene â€” it was a fabricated position.** `CelestialMarkers.tsx` plotted the NEO at a hardcoded `azimuthDeg: 310, altitudeDeg: 28` sourced from nothing: NeoWs provides only diameter/miss-distance/velocity (no RA/Dec, no alt/az â€” `API_SOURCES.md`), and `FORMULAS.md` Â§10 defines only diameter-from-H and miss-LD. This is exactly the fabricated-position class the Phase 7 Horizon Band NEO marker was removed for (entry 2026-07-23). Same resolution: NEO stays text-only (its Brief card), no sky-plotted marker. The PROGRESS.md claim "positioned at real Alt/Az coordinates derived from the Brief API" was false for this object.
- **ISS pass interpolation consolidated into `apps/web/src/lib/pass-interpolation.ts`** â€” `CelestialMarkers` had reimplemented `HorizonBand`'s pass interpolation with two divergences: (1) plain linear azimuth interpolation instead of shortest-path wrap, so a north-crossing pass (350Â°â†’10Â°) would sweep 340Â° the wrong way around the dome; (2) outside the pass window it showed the ISS anyway, parked at the pass-max position with a 5Â° altitude floor â€” a fabricated "current position" (the documented stance, in `HorizonBand`'s own comment, is: no visible pass â†’ no marker). Both components now import the one shared implementation (`interpolatePassPosition`, verbatim extraction of `HorizonBand`'s correct logic), and the /explore ISS marker only appears during an active visible pass, driven by `ExplorePage`'s 60s scene clock instead of a single `Date.now()` frozen at brief-load. Consequence, accepted: the /explore ISS marker is absent most of the time â€” that is the honest state, consistent with the Horizon Band. Kept in `apps/web/src/lib` (not `packages/shared`) because it interpolates the web wire payload's three N2YO sample points â€” presentation-layer plumbing, not an engine; `packages/shared`'s Â§5 `satellite-pass` engine is the backend's real pass computation and stays untouched.
- **Jupiter marker color `brass-300` (`#C9B187`) â€” accepted, now logged.** DESIGN_SPEC.md's celestial color table defines `solar`, `orbital`, `aurora`, `neo` â€” no Jupiter/planet token; `brass-300`'s documented role is "tick marks and scale rules." Both `HorizonBand` (Phase 7, `bg-brass-300`) and `CelestialMarkers` (this task) use it for Jupiter, consistently with each other. Accepted as the deliberate choice for planets until the human either blesses it or adds a dedicated token to the spec; flagged rather than silently changed. ISS (`#A8B4BC` = `orbital`) and Sun (`#D9A05B` = `solar`) match the token table exactly.
- **Sun marker azimuth 180Â° placeholder now also lives in /explore** â€” `CelestialMarkers` repeats `HorizonBand`'s hardcoded due-South Sun azimuth (real altitude, placeholder azimuth). Covered by the existing 2026-07-23 deviation entry; the proper fix remains backend `sunAzimuthDeg` exposure (tracked follow-up). Noted here so the extension of that deviation to a second surface is on the record, not silent.

## 2026-07-24 â€” /explore Sun azimuth placeholder retired in CelestialMarkers + HeliospherePulse (Claude Code)

The 2026-07-23 backend follow-up (`skyAnchor.data.sunAzimuthDeg`, non-nullable) is now wired into both /explore consumers that touch the Sun's position.

- **`CelestialMarkers.tsx`'s Sun marker** now reads `brief.skyAnchor.data.sunAzimuthDeg` instead of the hardcoded `azimuthDeg: 180`. Added the matching `sunAzimuthDeg: number` field to the frontend's `SkyAnchorCardData` type (`apps/web/src/lib/api.ts`), which had only ever declared `sunAltitudeDeg` â€” the type was stale relative to the backend card.
- **`HeliospherePulse.tsx` previously did NOT carry the 180Â° placeholder itself** â€” it independently recomputed the Sun's true azimuth/altitude client-side every render via the shared `sunHorizontalPosition()` engine call, given `observerLat`/`observerLon`/`currentTime`. That was a second, divergent source of Sun geometry: the wash's origin (real, live-computed) and the marker dot's position (180Â° placeholder) disagreed with each other in the same scene. (PROGRESS.md's 2026-07-24 Antigravity verification entry describing this as "the Sun az placeholder is still 180Â°" was imprecise â€” the placeholder lived only in the marker; the pulse's direction was already correct, just computed independently.) Resolved by retiring the independent recompute: `HeliospherePulse` now takes a `brief: DailyBrief | null` prop (replacing `observerLat`/`observerLon`/`currentTime`, which had no other use in the component) and reads the identical `brief.skyAnchor.data.sunAzimuthDeg`/`sunAltitudeDeg` fields `CelestialMarkers` reads â€” same object path, same source, so the wash and the marker dot can no longer drift apart. `sunHorizontalPosition` import dropped from the component.
- **Fallback, made identical rather than duplicated:** neither consumer invents a placeholder number anymore. Both null-check the same optional chain (`brief?.skyAnchor?.data?.sun...Deg ?? null` / `brief.skyAnchor?.data`) and simply don't render when it's missing â€” `CelestialMarkers` omits the Sun marker from its list; `HeliospherePulse` folds the null check into its existing "no data â†’ no pulse" honesty gate (alongside `solarWindSpeedKmS`), so a brief that hasn't loaded yet produces an invisible wash rather than one washing from due South.
- **`HorizonBand.tsx`'s Sun marker still uses the 180Â° placeholder** â€” explicitly out of scope for this task (per the 2026-07-23 PROGRESS.md entry assigning that wiring to a future Antigravity task) and left untouched.
- âœ… Gates green (real runs): `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/web/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on all four touched files (`apps/web/src/lib/api.ts`, `CelestialMarkers.tsx`, `HeliospherePulse.tsx`, `ExplorePage.tsx`) after one `prettier --write` pass on `HeliospherePulse.tsx`. No web test suite exists; shared engines untouched.

## 2026-07-24 â€” Phase 8 semantic zoom / Rule of 7 + Â§11 camera-rig rows 0:20 and 0:40 (Claude Code)

The Â§11 Rule-of-7 aggregation system and the two unbuilt opening-sequence camera behaviors. Design decisions, invented constants (flagged), and honest-scope boundaries:

- **The aggregation engine is pure presentation policy in `apps/web/src/lib/semantic-zoom.ts`** â€” no physics, no FORMULAS.md constants â€” beside `pass-interpolation.ts`, not in `packages/shared`. Discrete zoom levels (three FOV bands over CameraController's 30â€“90Â° range, edges 45/70) rather than a continuous threshold: Â§11 itself says "at any zoom **level**", and discrete bands give free hysteresis â€” aggregation recomputes only when the camera crosses a band edge, never per frame.
- **The Rule of 7 is the trigger, not proximity.** At â‰¤ 7 total objects nothing aggregates (today's real roster of â‰¤ 3 passes through untouched at every level). Over the cap, unpinned satellites cluster by union-find connected components under a zoom-scaled angular radius; if the component count still busts the budget the radius widens Ã—1.5 up to 3 times; failing that, everything collapses into the single orbital-shell glow. Enforcement is scene-wide (all-sky), strictly stronger than Â§11's per-view wording â€” the engine sees zoom level, not camera direction.
- **Two invented presentation constants, flagged for review** (same class as the Heliosphere Pulse's windâ†’period mapping): merge radius = **0.15 Ã— band-midpoint FOV** (two satellites within ~15% of view height read as one thing), and **max cluster extent 15Â°** centroid-to-member (~30Â° across â€” wider than a large real constellation stops reading as one asterism). The extent cap exists because union-find chaining can link an evenly-spread population into one sky-wide "cluster" that satisfies the cap while being illegible â€” caught by the unit tests, not review. Since widening only grows extent, an over-extended component at any step escalates straight to the shell.
- **The ISS is pinned** (never clustered): it is Â§11's hero object â€” the opening tether, cursor gravity, and the 0:20 camera lock all address it individually. Pinned objects still count toward the 7.
- **Cluster/shell interaction is drill-in, not a panel**: clicking an aggregate flies the camera to it and zooms one band in (cinematic tween), where it resolves into finer structure â€” that loop _is_ the semantic zoom. Individual objects keep the Â§11 tethered panel unchanged. The shell's dome glow is ambient (no handlers, never steals clicks); its one clickable is a labeled handle at the member centroid, altitude floored to 15Â° â€” presentation only, the glow itself is the whole dome.
- **Constellation glyph is real geometry**: member dots at their true positions joined by 1px brass hairlines to the clickable core â€” the shape is the actual sky configuration, not an icon. Labels via `DiegeticText` (auto-mirrored for screen readers). Regime changes crossfade at Â§7.1's transition class (400ms expo.out) â€” departing renderables stay mounted briefly and fade out.
- **First web-side test harness**: `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` + `test` script (vitest is already a root devDependency â€” no new dependency). The engine ships with 24 tests including a property-style invariant sweep (seeded populations 0â€“100 Ã— 3 levels Ã— 4 seeds: clickables â‰¤ 7 always; every satellite accounted for in some renderable â€” "aggregates rather than hides" verified literally). This also unblocks future tests for `pass-interpolation.ts`.
- **Dev-only simulated population (`?simSats=N`, DEV builds only)** in `lib/dev-sim-satellites.ts`: the payloads carry no satellite beyond the ISS (CelesTrak's client exists server-side but feeds no poller product), so the cluster/shell regimes are unreachable in production until the "clickable ISS/satellites" workstream lands (CelesTrak group + client-side satellite.js propagation â€” both already named in ARCHITECTURE.md/API_SOURCES.md). Shipping a fake production population would break the no-fictional-objects rule; a deterministic, explicitly-SIMULATED-labeled rig gated behind `import.meta.env.DEV` is a test instrument, not a product claim. Production builds tree-shake it to nothing.
- **Â§11 0:20 "rise from ground to orbit" is implemented as a ground-view cinematic lock** â€” a 1.6s expo.inOut yaw/pitch/FOV tween onto the ISS (the stated duration/ease, via `OPENING_SEQUENCE`), then per-frame follow as the ISS moves along its pass (fed by the same shared `interpolatePassPosition` the marker uses â€” one source of truth; pass ends mid-lock â†’ quiet release). The literal orbit flight with "Earth resolves" needs the orbit view that still doesn't exist â€” same documented deferral as the Auroral Ring's ground-view reading. Lock FOV = level 0's midpoint (37.5Â°).
- **Â§11 0:40 "the user drags, camera breaks lock"** â€” implemented on pointerdown **and wheel** (wheel is camera motion the user takes; a lock fighting the wheel would read as a stuck camera). Reduced motion: the cinematic collapses to Â§7.6's 200ms linear move. Closing the ISS panel also releases the lock; breaking the lock leaves the panel open (Â§11's 0:40 keeps the story on screen).
- **The scripted opening-sequence choreography at 0:12/0:20/0:26 remains pass-dependent**: those rows only literally occur when a visible ISS pass is live during the first visit â€” there is no honest "point of light begins moving" otherwise. What shipped is the _mechanics_ those rows run on (click â†’ rise-and-lock, drag â†’ break); the timing table stays in `motion.ts` for the choreographed first-visit path when a pass is actually up. Cursor gravity (0:12) and the three-depth hold interaction remain unbuilt â€” separate tasks.

## 2026-07-24 â€” Phase 8 backend prerequisite: real satellite population (CelesTrak TLE elements only, propagation stays client-side, by Claude Code)

Built the backend half of "the clustering engine has no real population to cluster" â€” the outstanding item every prior Phase 8 log entry (2026-07-24, semantic-zoom entry above; PROGRESS.md's matching entry) flagged as the plural "clickable ISS/satellites" work. **Deviates from the task as literally worded** in one deliberate way, flagged before building rather than after, per this file's own stated purpose:

1. **Propagation-locus conflict, resolved by asking rather than guessing.** The task asked for satellite.js to run on the backend and compute real-time alt/az per satellite. But `ARCHITECTURE.md` Â§2's locked stack table pins "Orbital propagation (client-side): satellite.js" â€” not backend â€” and this exact split ("CelesTrak group + **client-side** satellite.js propagation") is already named in `dev-sim-satellites.ts`'s own doc comment and in three separate PROGRESS.md/DECISIONS.md entries from this same Phase 8 workstream. Alt/az is also inherently observer-location- and time-dependent, which fits neither the central-poller model (no single "the" observer) nor a sane per-request cadence for a continuously-orbiting, continuously-panning-camera 3D scene â€” the same reason star positions (`equatorialToHorizontal`) are computed client-side per-frame rather than server-side, and unlike the Jupiter/Sun azimuth precedent (backend `equatorialToHorizontal`), which is a single per-request snapshot for the non-interactive 2D Brief, not a live scene. Flagged to the human via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code (CLAUDE.md: "don't silently override [a locked doc]... flag it to the human"); confirmed: **backend exposes only raw, validated orbital elements; satellite.js propagation to alt/az remains a client-side follow-up**, out of this task's scope.
2. **Subset criterion: CelesTrak's own `GROUP=visual`** (`apps/api/src/poller/slow-tier.ts`'s `SATELLITE_GROUP`) â€” CelesTrak's own curated "visually notable"/naked-eye-visible list (~100-160 objects, incl. ISS), not an invented selection rule. This is a literal match for the task's own suggested criterion ("a curated 'visually notable' list") and API_SOURCES.md's "bounded, meaningful slice" intent, while staying far under "tens of thousands." Defensive `MAX_SATELLITES = 200` cap added on top (matches the frontend's own `?simSats` ceiling in `dev-sim-satellites.ts`) in case the upstream group ever grows past a sane count for a night-sky scene.
3. **Cache tier: the existing slow tier (5-15min), not a new tier.** API_SOURCES.md already classifies CelesTrak as "slow tier, cached" in its Rate-Budget Summary, and the task itself says "this is slow-tier, not a per-request fetch" â€” so `fetchCelestrakTle` was added as a sixth client to `slow-tier.ts`'s existing `Promise.allSettled`, mirroring exactly how the Jupiter Horizons ephemeris was added to this same loop (2026-07-23 entry above: "extended not duplicated"). A dedicated, much-longer-lived poll tier was considered (TLEs are valid for hours/days) but rejected: `ARCHITECTURE.md` Â§4 is explicitly titled "Two-Tier Polling" and lists CelesTrak nowhere in either tier's description â€” introducing a third tier would be a second, unforced deviation from a locked doc, and the central-poller principle already delivers CelesTrak's "cache-encouraged" ask in full at any reasonable interval (one fetch per 10min regardless of user count). Failure policy mirrors Horizons/DONKI: a failed fetch preserves the last known-good element set, marked unhealthy â€” matches API_SOURCES.md's documented "use last cached TLE set... only if never fetched does satellite rendering degrade."
4. **Extended the Phase 1 CelesTrak client, did not rebuild it.** Added a sibling `fetchCelestrakTle` (FORMAT=tle) next to the existing `fetchCelestrakOmm` (FORMAT=json) in `apps/api/src/clients/celestrak/` â€” same endpoint, same retry/timeout pipeline (`fetchWithRetry` generalized to accept a response parser, defaulting to `.json()` so `fetchCelestrakOmm`'s behavior is unchanged), only the upstream `FORMAT` and response shape differ. Chose FORMAT=tle over reusing the OMM/JSON path because satellite.js's public `twoline2satrec(line1, line2)` API expects the classic fixed-column two-line text verbatim; there is no supported way to hand it structured OMM fields directly without re-deriving TLE line/checksum formatting ourselves (an unnecessary reimplementation CelesTrak already solves for free via one query-param change).
5. **Validation is structural, not checksum-based** (`CelestrakTleRecordSchema`): exact 69-char line length, correct `1 `/`2 ` line-number marker, and â€” a genuinely useful cross-check beyond pure shape â€” matching NORAD catalog number between `line1` and `line2`. Deliberately does **not** verify the TLE's arithmetic checksum digit: every other Phase 1 client validates upstream _shape_ via Zod, not upstream _arithmetic_, and satellite.js's own `twoline2satrec` doesn't verify checksums either â€” matching the project's existing validation depth rather than adding a new, heavier standard for just this one source.
6. **ISS is filtered out of the new `/api/satellites` payload** (`apps/api/src/routes/satellites.ts`, filters `noradCatId === 25544`), even though CelesTrak's `visual` group includes it. The product already has a dedicated, more authoritative, live N2YO-sourced ISS position (`brief/iss-card.ts`), and the semantic-zoom system's pinned-ISS concept (`apps/web/src/lib/semantic-zoom.ts`) is built around one hero object. Exposing a second, TLE-propagated ISS position here would let the two disagree â€” the same class of bug the 2026-07-24 Sun-position consolidation entry above fixed (two divergent sources for the same body). Flagged here as a proactive application of that same lesson, not something a review caught after the fact.
7. **Payload shape is deliberately NOT `SkyObjectInput`** (`apps/web/src/lib/semantic-zoom.ts`'s consumed type). Per decision 1, this endpoint cannot produce alt/az, so it was never a candidate for that shape. `/api/satellites` returns `{ satellites: SatelliteElementSet[], fetchedAt, healthy }` where `SatelliteElementSet = { id, name, line1, line2 }` â€” `id` is the NORAD catalog number as a string (stable key), `line1`/`line2` are the raw two-line elements a future client-side `satellite.js` propagator needs as its direct input. Building that propagator (satellite.js â†’ ECI â†’ `packages/shared`'s existing alt/az transform or `satellite.js`'s own look-angle helpers â†’ `SkyObjectInput[]`) is the explicit, documented follow-up, left for the frontend session per this task's own scope ("Phase 8 backend prerequisite").
8. **Wiring:** new `satellites` slot in `poller/store.ts`'s `PollerState`; new sixth client in `slow-tier.ts`; wired into `index.ts`'s composition root; surfaced in `/health` like every other source; new `/api/satellites` GET route registered in `app.ts`.
9. **Gates, real runs:** `npx tsc --build --force` (repo-wide) exit 0; `npx eslint apps/api/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `npx prettier --check` clean on all 17 touched/new files (one `--write` pass needed on 5 of them); `npx vitest run` (apps/api) â€” 350/350 passing, including the Phase 3 orchestration integration test (updated for the new sixth slow-tier client) and `build-brief.test.ts`'s hand-built `PollerState` fixture (updated for the new `satellites` field).

**Not done (explicitly out of scope, tracked):** the client-side `satellite.js` propagator that turns `SatelliteElementSet[]` + observer lat/lon/time into `SkyObjectInput[]` for `semantic-zoom.ts` to cluster. WORKPLAN.md's `Current Phase` marker is left at Phase 8 (unchanged) â€” this is a prerequisite, not phase closure.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Phase 8: Client-side satellite propagation (coordinate transform, recompute interval, culling)

**Why:** Three calls made while implementing the client-side `satellite.js` propagator in `apps/web/src/hooks/useSatellites.ts`:

1. **Coordinate Transform:** Used `satellite.js`'s native `eciToEcf` and `ecfToLookAngles` instead of `packages/shared`'s `equatorialToHorizontal`. The shared engine converts RA/Dec (equatorial), but SGP4 outputs Earth-Centered Inertial (ECI) coordinates. Converting ECI to ECF (Earth-Centered Earth-Fixed) and then to topocentric look angles (alt/az) natively factors in Earth's oblateness, which is the standard and correct frame for satellite propagation.
2. **Recompute Interval (2000ms):** SGP4 propagation for ~200 satellites is fast but not zero-cost. Throttling the update to once every 2 seconds (`PROPAGATE_INTERVAL_MS = 2000`) avoids a per-frame CPU tax while keeping positions visually accurate enough for the semantic zoom's clustering.
3. **No Below-Horizon Culling:** Satellites with `altitudeDeg < 0` are explicitly NOT filtered out by the hook. This matches the existing convention in `CelestialMarkers.tsx` for the Sun and ISS, where the `SkyDome` geometry physically obscures them. Leaving them in preserves the semantic aggregation behavior below the geometric horizon without abrupt popping when clusters cross the boundary.

**Correction, 2026-07-27 (see the verification entry below): the "SkyDome physically obscures them" claim in this entry and in the hook's own comment was false.** `SkyDome.tsx` is a thin decorative `ringGeometry(998, 1000)` horizon hairline, not a ground plane or dome â€” there is nothing behind it to occlude anything. Below-horizon Sun/ISS/satellite markers currently render fully unclipped. Not a new bug introduced by this entry's work (the Sun/ISS behavior already predated it and was already unclipped) â€” but the rationale as written was never actually true and is corrected here rather than left standing.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Independent verification of Antigravity's client-side satellite propagator: three real defects found and fixed (Claude Code)

**Why logged:** Antigravity's self-report (PROGRESS.md, same date) couldn't run any gates itself (sandbox blocked node/npm) and repeated the "SkyDome clips these visually" claim above without checking it. Per `CLAUDE.md`'s "verify yourself" rule, ran every gate for real (after discovering `satellite.js` was declared in `package.json` but never actually `npm install`ed â€” no lock-file entry, no `node_modules` folder), plus an independent ground-truth propagation check. Found three real, shippable-breaking defects beyond what the self-report could have caught, and confirmed the propagation math itself was always correct.

1. **`/explore` crashed to a broken, endlessly-erroring scene on every load (highest severity).** `useSatellites.ts` read `useAppStore`'s `state.location` directly and dereferenced `loc.lat`/`loc.lon` â€” including inside a `useEffect` dependency array (`[tleData, loc.lat, loc.lon]`), evaluated synchronously during every render of `CelestialMarkers` â€” with no null guard. `state.location` is `null` by default, and **no code path anywhere in `apps/web/src` ever calls `setLocation`** (confirmed via repo-wide grep: zero `setLocation(` call sites outside the store's own definition, zero `navigator.geolocation` references, no `persist` middleware on the store) â€” the store's own comment ("null until geolocation or user selects a saved location") describes wiring that doesn't exist yet. Reproduced live in a real browser (`npx vite`, headless Chrome, `/explore`): `TypeError: Cannot read properties of null (reading 'lat')` thrown from `useSatellites`, caught by `@react-three/fiber`'s internal Canvas error boundary, which retries and fails identically every time, ending in `THREE.WebGLRenderer: Context Lost` â€” the entire 3D scene (stars, Sun, Jupiter, ISS, everything, not just satellites) breaks and never recovers, on every single page load. **Fixed:** a real `if (loc === null) { setPropagated([]); return; }` early-return in the propagation effect; the effect's dependency array changed from the crash-prone `[tleData, loc.lat, loc.lon]` to `[tleData, loc]` (so it correctly re-runs and starts propagating the moment `loc` becomes non-null, with no other code change needed later). Reproduced the crash live pre-fix and confirmed its absence post-fix, both via real browser console output, not just static reasoning. **Not fixed, deliberately:** the underlying missing-location-wiring gap. `getEffectiveLocation()` (`apps/web/src/lib/api.ts`) already works around this same `null` everywhere else in the app via a Srinagar default, but baking that same fallback into `useSatellites.ts` was rejected â€” it would silently mask the fact that `state.location` is never populated by any real flow, rather than surface it. Flagging here as its own tracked, pre-existing gap, not a satellite-feature bug: **no component reads a real user location today; every "observer position" in the app is either the Srinagar default or requires a location-switcher UI/geolocation call that doesn't exist yet.** Whoever builds that wiring closes this gap for every consumer at once, including this hook, with no further change needed here.

2. **The real satellite feed was permanently dead code, in every configuration.** `CelestialMarkers.tsx`'s `satelliteObjects` gated on `simSatCountFromSearch(window.location.search) !== null` â€” but `simSatCountFromSearch` returns a plain `number` (never `null`; `0` means disabled), so the comparison was a tautology, always `true`. The `return realSats.map(...)` branch below it was unreachable dead code: real satellites from `/api/satellites` could never render, with or without `?simSats=N`, in dev or production. Not caught by `tsc` (`number === null` does not trigger TS2367 under this project's own `--strict` config â€” verified directly against a minimal repro rather than assumed) or by ESLint (no active rule flags a numeric-vs-null tautology). **Fixed:** extracted the decision into a pure, exported `resolveSatelliteFeed(search, simObjects, realObjects)` (`apps/web/src/lib/dev-sim-satellites.ts`), using `simSatCountFromSearch(search) > 0` â€” real satellites now win whenever `simSats` is absent/zero/invalid, even if the real feed happens to be the only non-empty list. Regression-tested directly (`dev-sim-satellites.test.ts`): asserts the real feed is selected and non-empty with no `simSats` param, so this exact class of bug (a tautological guard silently killing a whole feature) can't ship unnoticed again.

3. **`tsc`/ESLint failures, root-caused to an upstream `satellite.js` typing gap, not the propagation logic.** `satellite.js` v5.0.0 genuinely ships its own hand-written types (`types/index.d.ts`, confirmed via its own `package.json`'s `types` field â€” not an `any` fallback), but that file omits `radiansToDegrees`/`radiansLat`/`radiansLong` despite all three being real, working runtime exports (`lib/transforms.js`) that `useSatellites.ts` calls â€” 2 `tsc` "property does not exist" errors, cascading into 4 ESLint `no-unsafe-*` errors (the untyped call resolves to an error type under type-aware linting). Verified the runtime call itself was always correct: cross-checked the exact, unmodified hook logic against an independent SGP4 implementation (Python `skyfield`, a different codebase from `satellite.js`) for two real CelesTrak `visual`-group satellites at an identical fixed instant â€” altitude agreed to ~0.0002Â°, azimuth to ~0.0008Â°. **Fixed** with a local module-augmentation declaration (`apps/web/src/types/satellite.js.d.ts`, same established pattern as this directory's existing `troika-three-text.d.ts`) adding the three missing signatures â€” not `any`/`@ts-ignore`, so the rest of the codebase keeps real type safety on this library. Also removed one genuinely unnecessary type assertion the same typing gap had prompted (`positionAndVelocity.position as satellite.EciVec3<number> | boolean` â€” `Kilometer` already is `number`).

4. **Below-horizon "SkyDome clipping" claim corrected** â€” see the amendment added above to the original 2026-07-27 entry. Logging the underlying gap here since it's now confirmed, not just suspected: **no component anywhere in `apps/web/src/components/explore` occludes sub-horizon markers.** Closing it needs either a real ground/dome occluder mesh or moving the "no culling" story onto something that actually clips (e.g. a shader discard in `SkyDome` keyed on world-space altitude) â€” not implemented here, tracked as a separate follow-up.

Gates re-run for real after all fixes: `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/web/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on every touched/new file; `vitest run` (apps/web) 31/31 passing (24 pre-existing + 7 new in `dev-sim-satellites.test.ts`). Live-browser check: pre-fix crash reproduced and confirmed fixed post-fix, `/explore` loads cleanly with `state.location` null (today's only real-world case) â€” full on-screen rendering of the real (non-simulated) satellite population was not verified live, since `/api/satellites` needs a running backend (Docker Postgres + `NASA_API_KEY` + `N2YO_API_KEY`, none available in this sandbox) â€” that remains a real-browser/real-backend check, same as cursor gravity and the three-depth hold interaction.

## 2026-07-27 â€” `DEFAULT_OBSERVER_LOCATION` changes permanently: Srinagar â†’ Jammu (32.73Â°N, 74.87Â°E)

**Why:** Srinagar (34.08Â°N, 74.80Â°E) was an arbitrary early placeholder â€” never a deliberate product choice, just the first plausible Kashmir-region coordinate picked when `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts`'s fallback-location chain was first written. Jammu is now the **ratified, permanent** default, confirmed by the project owner. `api.ts`'s `DEFAULT_OBSERVER_LOCATION`/`getEffectiveLocation()` edit (already made, uncommitted, by Antigravity) is correct and stays as-is â€” this entry just records the decision and finishes propagating it.

- **`DESIGN_SPEC.md`'s two locked example lines updated** (Â§10 Daily Brief eyebrow strip, Â§11 Explorable Universe opening-sequence 0:00 line) to Jammu's name/coordinates. The Brief eyebrow strip's `CIVIL TWILIGHT ENDS` time was also recomputed, not just left at the old number with new coordinates spliced in: checking the original Srinagar line against the project's own FORMULAS.md Â§3/Â§4 Meeus low-precision transform (same formula `packages/shared/src/engines/sun-position.ts` implements) for `WED 22 JUL` (2026 â€” confirmed an actual Wednesday) shows civil twilight there actually ends **~20:05 IST**, not the spec's stated `19:48` â€” cross-checked independently against Python `skyfield` (20:07 IST, ~2min agreement, consistent with the Meeus method's own ~0.01Â° accuracy bound), so the original figure was never a rigorous computation, just plausible-sounding placeholder flavor text. Jammu's civil twilight end for the same date, same method: **20:01 IST** (skyfield cross-check: 20:03) â€” that's the new line's value, genuinely computed rather than eyeballed. The opening-sequence line's `21:04 LOCAL` has no formula behind it (an arbitrary clock reading for the mockup) â€” only its coordinates changed.
- **Backend engine tests are untouched in substance** (`sun-position.test.ts`, `sky-anchor-card.test.ts`) â€” their hardcoded `34.08`/`74.8` fixtures test the shared math engines generically over arbitrary lat/lon, not the app's actual default, so they don't need to track this change at all. Added a one-line comment at each file's first use of the fixture, clarifying it's arbitrary and not tied to `DEFAULT_OBSERVER_LOCATION`, so a future reader isn't confused by the 34.08/74.8-vs-32.73/74.87 mismatch.
- **Per-user configurable default locations remain Phase 10 scope** â€” `SCHEMA.md`'s `Location` model already exists but no settings UI reads/writes it yet. This change only affects the no-saved-location fallback every logged-out (and not-yet-configured logged-in) user gets until then; it is not a statement about per-user defaults, which stay a future Phase 10 task.

## 2026-07-27 â€” `/explore` real horizon culling: Jupiter/Sun/ISS now hidden below the horizon; satellites gated on the same visible-pass test the ISS uses

**Why:** Live check confirmed `CelestialMarkers.tsx` rendered every anchor object (Jupiter, Sun, ISS) regardless of altitude â€” Jupiter at true altitude âˆ’36.5Â° (36Â° below the horizon) still showed as a bright clickable marker. Root cause for Jupiter specifically: `altitudeDeg: Math.max(2, jup.altitudeDeg)` silently floored its rendered position to at least 2Â°, so it could never visually sink below the ground no matter how far below the true horizon it actually was â€” not a missing check, an active one masking the problem. Sun and ISS had no floor, just no check at all.

- **Real culling, not a fade.** `apps/web/src/lib/semantic-zoom.ts` gets a new `isAboveHorizon(altitudeDeg)` (and its threshold, `HORIZON_REFRACTION_DEG`), used to gate whether Jupiter/Sun/ISS are pushed into `CelestialMarkers.tsx`'s object list at all. Chose a hard pre-filter over an opacity fade: objects that fail the check simply aren't in the list `aggregateSkyObjects` sees, and since the existing Â§7.1 transition-class crossfade already fades out anything that disappears between one aggregation and the next (tracked by `renderableKey`), a culled object gets the _same_ graceful ~400ms fade-out for free â€” no new fade machinery needed, and cull-driven disappearances read identically to a semantic-zoom regime change.
- **Refraction margin, not a hard 0Â° cutoff â€” deliberately real, not invented.** Used the standard atmospheric refraction at the horizon (34â€² â‰ˆ 0.5667Â°): a body's true geometric altitude can be slightly below the horizon and still be visually above it because the atmosphere bends grazing light â€” the same figure almanacs use for sunrise/sunset horizon dip, not a made-up buffer. `FORMULAS.md` doesn't govern this (checked â€” no mention of refraction anywhere in it), so it lives in `apps/web/src/lib/semantic-zoom.ts` as presentation policy, not `packages/shared`, matching the precedent already set for the Tâ†’RGB mapping (2026-07-15 entry). Jupiter's marker now uses its real altitude with no floor at all.
- **`StarField.tsx` is unchanged** â€” it has the identical no-culling gap (confirmed by reading it: relies on the same false "SkyDome clips it" premise as everything else), but it wasn't named in this task's scope and touching ~9000 stars' render path is a separate, larger piece of work. Flagged here so it isn't mistaken for already covered.
- **Satellites: gated on the exact same FORMULAS.md Â§5 visible-pass test the ISS already uses, reused not reimplemented.** `useSatellites.ts` was showing raw propagated positions with no illumination check at all â€” a satellite geometrically above the horizon but in Earth's shadow, or with the observer still in daylight, doesn't look like anything in reality, yet was rendered as a bright marker anyway. Checked `packages/shared` before writing anything new: `engines/satellite-pass.ts` already exports `isVisiblePass(elevationDeg, sunAltObserverDeg, satellitePosition, sunUnitVector)` â€” elevation â‰¥ 10Â° AND observer in darkness (Â§4's âˆ’6Â° threshold) AND the satellite outside Earth's shadow-cylinder (Â§5) â€” built for the ISS pass and equally valid for any satellite. Wired in directly: `positionEci` (already computed via `satellite.propagate`) is already in the right frame for `isVisiblePass`'s shadow test; the Sun's ECI-frame unit vector is derived from the existing `sunEquatorialPosition(jd)` (RA/Dec share ECI's equatorial reference frame, so the standard sphericalâ†’Cartesian conversion is a direct, correct match, not an approximation); observer darkness reuses `sunAltitudeDeg(now, loc.lat, loc.lon)`. All three per-tick, computed once (not per-satellite), same pattern as the existing `gmst`/`observerGd`.
- **`?simSats=N` simulated satellites are deliberately NOT illumination-filtered** â€” they're a dev-only rig for exercising semantic-zoom's cluster/shell regimes reliably at any time of day; gating them on real Sun/shadow geometry would make the rig flaky depending on when it's run, defeating its purpose.
- **New tests**: `semantic-zoom.test.ts` gains direct `isAboveHorizon` coverage (above, at the true horizon, within the refraction margin, and genuinely below it â€” including the exact âˆ’36.5Â° from the live bug report).
- Gates re-run for real: `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/web/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean; `vitest run` (apps/web) 34/34 passing (27 in `semantic-zoom.test.ts`, up from 24, + 7 in `dev-sim-satellites.test.ts`).
- **Not verified live in a real browser this time** â€” attempted a fetch-mocked live check (patching `window.fetch` for `/api/brief` and forcing an SPA remount via `history.pushState`) to visually confirm Jupiter disappears at âˆ’36.5Â°, but the mock `DailyBrief` payload was incomplete and crashed `BriefPage` (an unrelated route, hit only because the mocked fetch response applies repo-wide) before `/explore` could be observed cleanly. Rather than keep iterating on the mock harness, relied on the unit tests above (which exercise the exact same `isAboveHorizon`/`isVisiblePass` calls the components use) plus direct code reading. Flagging this as a real-browser check still owed, same class as the outstanding satellite-rendering verification already logged above.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Daily Brief `--surface` CSS custom property wired to real solar altitude

**Why:** `apps/web/src/index.css` previously statically set `--surface: var(--sky-900)` (night surface), with a Phase 7 follow-on comment noting that runtime solar-altitude interpolation was deferred. `BriefPage.tsx` already calculated `twilightState` via `twilightStateForSunAltitude(sunAltDeg)` from `brief.skyAnchor.data.sunAltitudeDeg`, but used it only for the eyebrow text label.

- **Reactive CSS custom property updates:** Added `useEffect` in `BriefPage.tsx` that computes `computeSurfaceColor(twilightState.value)` and `computeOnSurfaceColor(twilightState.value)` via a new helper module (`apps/web/src/lib/twilight-surface.ts`), reactively updating `document.documentElement.style.setProperty('--surface', ...)` and `document.documentElement.style.setProperty('--on-surface', ...)` whenever `twilightState.value` changes.
- **Continuous RGB interpolation across Twilight Stops:**
  - `value = 0` (Day, `sunAlt >= 0Â°`): `sky-100` (`#EEF1F1`, `rgb(238, 241, 241)`)
  - `value = 1` (Civil twilight end, `sunAlt = -6Â°`): `sky-400` (`#8B9898`, `rgb(139, 152, 152)`)
  - `value = 2` (Nautical twilight end, `sunAlt = -12Â°`): `sky-600` (`#3E4A4A`, `rgb(62, 74, 74)`)
  - `value = 3` (Night / Astronomical twilight end, `sunAlt <= -18Â°`): `sky-900` (`#111818`, `rgb(17, 24, 24)`)
    Linear interpolation smoothly shifts colors across continuous solar altitude changes. `--on-surface` is dynamically derived to guarantee WCAG 2.1 AA contrast ($\ge 4.5:1$ for normal text) at every point along the continuous ramp.
- **Unit tests added & WCAG 2.1 AA contrast verified (DESIGN_SPEC.md L548):** `apps/web/src/lib/twilight-surface.test.ts` computes exact relative luminance ($L$) and contrast ratios across all 4 boundary stops, 10 interpolated midpoints, and a 0.05 step sweep across $v \in [0, 3]$. Verified $\ge 4.5:1$ compliance at every continuous step (e.g. $17.26:1$ at day, $6.73:1$ at civil boundary, $4.59:1$ at mid-nautical transition, $7.85:1$ at nautical boundary, $15.54:1$ at night).

## 2026-07-27 â€” Backend JPL Horizons pipeline extended to Venus, Mars, Saturn, Mercury (same pattern as Jupiter)

Backend-only extension of the Sky Anchor's planet-marker pipeline (Task A/Task F's Jupiter precedent) to the four remaining classical planets. Frontend consumption is a deliberate, separate follow-up â€” nothing in `apps/web` was touched.

- **Command codes verified live against the real JPL Horizons API before wiring in, not assumed from the Jupiter pattern.** Fetched `COMMAND='199'/'299'/'499'/'699'` against `https://ssd.jpl.nasa.gov/api/horizons.api` and confirmed the response's own header line names Mercury/Venus/Mars/Saturn respectively (matching the standard, well-documented JPL major-body numbering â€” position-from-Sun Ã— 100 + 99 â€” that also explains Jupiter's existing `599`). Also confirmed the returned CSV ephemeris rows are byte-for-byte the same shape `parseEphemerisRaDecLines` already parses for Jupiter (`date, solar-presence, lunar-presence, RA_deg, Dec_deg,`) â€” no client changes needed, `fetchHorizonsRaDec` was already body-agnostic (`command` is a caller-supplied param).
- **`poller/store.ts`:** added `horizonsVenus`/`horizonsMars`/`horizonsSaturn`/`horizonsMercury` (`SourceState<HorizonsRaDecData>`), same shape as `horizonsJupiter`.
- **`poller/slow-tier.ts`:** `fetchHorizonsRaDec` now runs five times a tick (one call per body) instead of once. Consolidated the per-body write logic that used to be Jupiter-only (`writeHorizonsJupiterResult`) into a shared `writeHorizonsRaDecResult(key, data, nowIso)` plus a shared `handleHorizonsRaDecResult` for the `Promise.allSettled` fulfilled/rejected handling â€” the fallback logic (JPL Horizons's documented "serve last computed set," matching the existing Jupiter/Sun precedent) is identical for all five bodies, so five near-duplicate functions would have been pure duplication. Renamed `HORIZONS_JUPITER_STEP` â†’ `HORIZONS_PLANET_STEP` (still `'1 h'`) since it's now shared by all five.
- **`sky-anchor-card.ts`:** `buildSkyAnchorCard`'s 4th parameter changed from a single `jupiterEphemeris: SourceState<HorizonsRaDecData>` to a new `PlanetEphemerides` object (`{ jupiter, venus, mars, saturn, mercury }`). A 5-positional-argument signature of identically-typed `SourceState` params was rejected as a bad, mix-up-prone API the moment a second body needed to exist alongside Jupiter â€” a named-fields object is self-documenting and makes a future sixth body a one-line addition, not a signature reshuffle. `SkyAnchorCard` gains `venus`/`mars`/`saturn`/`mercury: HorizontalPosition | null`, each resolved through a shared `resolvePlanetPosition()` helper (nearest-ephemeris-row-to-`now`, same FORMULAS.md Â§3 transform) rather than the Jupiter block copy-pasted four times. Each body degrades to `null` fully independently â€” one body's missing/failed ephemeris never affects another's, verified directly in tests (`it.each` over all five bodies, asserting only the targeted body's ephemeris populates its own field).
- **`build-brief.ts`:** the `buildSkyAnchorCard` call site now assembles `PlanetEphemerides` from the poller state's five `horizons*` slots.
- **`health.ts`:** `buildHealthPayload`'s `sources` record (typed `Record<SourceKey, ...>`) required the four new keys at compile time the moment they were added to `PollerState` â€” TypeScript itself caught every call site needing an update, nothing was found by manual grep alone.
- **Tests:** extended rather than copy-pasted per body â€” `sky-anchor-card.test.ts` and `slow-tier.test.ts` both use `it.each`/`describe.each` over the five bodies (success resolves correctly, nearest-row selection, degrades to null with no prior data, preserves last-known-good on a later failure, an unexpected rejection is treated as a failure rather than thrown, and one body's failure never affects the other four) instead of hand-duplicating Jupiter's existing test shape four more times. `store.test.ts`, `build-brief.test.ts`, `health.test.ts`, and `phase3-orchestration.test.ts` updated for the four new `PollerState`/`HealthPayload` keys.
- Gates, real runs: `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/api/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean; `vitest run` (apps/api) â€” 38 files, 379 tests, all passing.
- **Not done (explicitly out of scope, per this task):** any `apps/web` consumption of `venus`/`mars`/`saturn`/`mercury` on `skyAnchor.data` â€” `CelestialMarkers.tsx` still only reads `jupiter`. Field names are confirmed here (`altitudeDeg`/`azimuthDeg`, matching Jupiter's shape exactly) precisely so that follow-up can wire them in without any backend renegotiation.

Only live-computed data (`ALT: ...Â° Â· AZ: ...Â°`) is displayed on planet panels. (ISS magnitude remains live from N2YO visual pass telemetry).

## 2026-07-27 â€” Cursor Gravity (Â§11 0:12) & Three-Depth Hold ("Peeling the Onion") Implementation

**Why:** `DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§11 specifies Cursor Gravity (40px magnetic pointer attraction with 60ms ease) and Three-Depth Hold ("Peeling the Onion": tap for sentence, hold for measurements, hold longer for full technical data).

- **Cursor Gravity (40px magnetic radius, 60ms ease):** Implemented in `CameraController.tsx` / `CelestialMarkers.tsx`. Tracks 2D screen positions of selectable objects. When the pointer moves within 40px of a selectable celestial marker, a smooth exponential 60ms lerp ($\alpha \approx 1 - e^{-\Delta t / 0.06}$) applies a subtle camera focal bias toward the object and activates raycast magnetic target snapping. During the opening sequence at 0:12 (`OPENING_SEQUENCE.issRevealAt`), gravity specifically biases toward the ISS.
- **Three-Depth Hold Duration Thresholds (Chosen Defaults):** `DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§11 specifies "hold" and "hold longer" without specifying exact millisecond numbers. Selected **500ms** (Depth 2: Measurements) and **1000ms** (Depth 3: Deep Link) as the hold-duration thresholds (logged as chosen engineering defaults, same precedent as `CameraController` damping/friction constants).
- **Progressive Disclosure UI & Accessibility:**
  - **Depth 1 (Tap / Short Click)**: Renders Header + Sentence (serif font).
  - **Depth 2 (Hold 500ms / Tap step 2)**: Progressively reveals Measurements (mono font).
  - **Depth 3 (Hold 1000ms / Tap step 3)**: Progressively reveals Deep Link (`linkText` $\rightarrow$ `linkHref`).
  - Added explicit interactive depth step buttons (`[1] [2] [3]`) to ensure WCAG AA accessibility for screen readers and keyboard users.

| Instant (UTC)    | Î” ecliptic longitude | Î” ecliptic latitude | Î” distance |
| ---------------- | -------------------- | ------------------- | ---------- |
| 2000-01-01T12:00 | 0.200Â°               | 0.205Â°              | 109 km     |
| 2026-07-27T06:41 | 0.216Â°               | 0.018Â°              | 148 km     |
| 2026-08-12T00:00 | 0.307Â°               | 0.005Â°              | 186 km     |

Illuminated-fraction cross-check (elongation approximation vs skyfield's true phase-angle method, both using this engine's own computed RA/Dec): agreed within 0.001â€“0.003 (0.1â€“0.3 percentage points) at all three dates â€” confirms Meeus ch. 48's own claim that the simpler elongation method is an adequate stand-in for the true phase angle when Earth-Sun distance vastly exceeds Earth-Moon distance. These numbers, not a guess, are what FORMULAS.md Â§12's accuracy claim is based on â€” the ~0.2â€“0.3Â° residual is consistent with this truncation's well-documented accuracy, confirming the recalled coefficients were transcribed correctly rather than silently degraded.

**Rise/set cross-checked at the full-pipeline level, not just the position level.** Compared `nextMoonRiseSet(32.73, 74.87, ...)` against skyfield's `almanac.risings_and_settings` for the same observer (Jammu), first with skyfield's default -34' refraction threshold (5min/1m42s off â€” expected, since Â§12 explicitly specifies a _geometric_ 0Â° crossing with no refraction, unlike skyfield's default), then re-run with `horizon_degrees=0` for a true apples-to-apples geometric comparison: rise differed by 8m18s, set by 1m36s. This timing gap is a direct, expected consequence of the ~0.2â€“0.3Â° position accuracy already established above â€” a small altitude error near a shallow horizon-crossing angle amplifies into several minutes of timing error; it is not a separate bug in the bisection solver. Logged here so a future reader doesn't mistake an 8-minute gap against a stricter reference for a regression.

**Implementation choices, each reusing existing pieces rather than reimplementing:**

- `sun-position.ts` gained two small additive exports â€” `sunEclipticLongitudeDeg(jd)` and `obliquityOfEclipticDeg(jd)` â€” refactored out of the existing (unchanged-behavior) `sunEquatorialPosition`, so the Moon's phase-name computation and equatorial-position rotation reuse the Sun's own already-tested intermediate values instead of a second copy. Verified behavior-preserving: `sun-position.test.ts`'s existing assertions pass unchanged.
- `moonHorizontalPosition`/`moonAltitudeDeg` reuse `equatorialToHorizontal` (Â§3) exactly as the Sun does â€” no new alt/az transform.
- `nextMoonRiseSet` reuses `bisectionSolve` (`math-utils.ts`, the same generic solver Â§6's CME arrival uses) unmodified: a coarse forward scan brackets each 0Â°-crossing, then the existing solver refines it. Set-crossings bisect on `-altitude` (not a separate solver) since `bisectionSolve` is written for increasing functions only.
- `moonPhaseName` exposed as its own function (not inlined) specifically so its 8 bin boundaries are testable with exact synthetic angles rather than needing to reverse-engineer real dates that happen to produce them.
