## 2026-07-27 â€” `apps/api` coverage threshold is global, not per-file; `brief.test.ts` float8 round-trip flake logged

Two corrections to the record while closing the Phase 8 gap-audit item on `n2yo`/`nasa` client branch coverage.

**1. `apps/api/vitest.config.ts`'s `thresholds` block is global, not per-file.** PROGRESS.md's Phase 3 entry (line 124) records `apps/api`'s local `test:coverage` as _failing_ its 80% branch threshold "on several Phase-1 clients." That framing has since drifted: the thresholds apply to the aggregate `All files` row, so with the suite grown to 38 files the gate has actually been exiting 0 for a while â€” verified directly by running `npx vitest run --coverage` on a `git stash`ed tree (exit 0, `All files` branch 91.73%). What was true is that `n2yo.client.ts` (73.91%) and `nasa.client.ts` (41.66%) sat below 80% branch _individually_. Both are now at 100% and 98.38% respectively. No threshold was weakened and no file was excluded; the fix was tests only.

**2. `nasa.client.ts:136-138` left uncovered, deliberately.** That is `fetchNasaDonki`'s outer `try/catch` â€” unreachable in practice, because both `fetchWithRetry` calls already have their own `.catch` handlers attached inside the `Promise.all`, so no real fetch/timeout/HTTP/parse failure can escape to it. Reaching it requires injecting a throw into `console.error` or a poisoned proxy payload, neither of which tests any real behavior. Same reasoning already applied to `fast-tier.ts`/`slow-tier.ts`'s outer `.catch` log lines in Phase 3.

**3. Pre-existing flake found** (was flagged here as out of scope for the coverage task; **fixed in the follow-up below**): `apps/api/src/routes/brief.test.ts:306`, `expect(stored[0]?.confidence).toBe(body.spaceWeather.data?.aurora?.confidence)`, fails intermittently:

```
AssertionError: expected 0.0385884633329388 to be 0.038588463332938805
```

The received value is the Postgres round-trip, the expected value is the in-process double. `Prediction.confidence` is a Prisma `Float` (PG `double precision`); the value comes back rounded to 15 significant digits, so exact `.toBe()` equality holds only when the computed confidence happens to need â‰¤15 digits. Confirmed pre-existing and independent of this task: it reproduces running `brief.test.ts` **alone** on a `git stash`ed tree (1 failure in 3 consecutive runs, same value each time). The fix is a precision-tolerant assertion (`toBeCloseTo`) rather than exact equality on a DB-round-tripped float, but that belongs to whoever owns the predictions-persistence tests, not to a coverage task â€” logging rather than silently editing an unrelated test.

## 2026-07-27 â€” `brief.test.ts` prediction-persistence floats asserted with `toBeCloseTo(â€¦, 12)`, precision chosen from measurement

Follow-up that fixes item 3 of the entry above. The two `.toBe()` assertions on `Prediction.predictedKp`/`confidence` compared a Postgres-round-tripped `Float` against the in-process double, which is not an equality that holds in general.

**The tolerance was measured against the real database, not guessed.** A throwaway probe wrote 800 random doubles through Prisma into the actual docker-compose Postgres and read them back â€” 400 in `confidence`'s range [0, 1] and 400 in `predictedKp`'s range [0, 9], plus the exact value from the observed failure:

```
server extra_float_digits = 1
server version = PostgreSQL 16.14 (Debian 16.14-1.pgdg13+1)

--- confidence (n=401)
  exact round-trips: 297/401 (74.1%)
  max ABSOLUTE error: 5.5511e-17
  max RELATIVE error: 4.3829e-16
  worst case sent: 0.18375882257757015
  worst case got : 0.1837588225775701

--- predictedKp (n=400)
  exact round-trips: 282/400 (70.5%)
  max ABSOLUTE error: 8.8818e-16
  max RELATIVE error: 4.0681e-16
  worst case sent: 4.1504847811033905
  worst case got : 4.15048478110339
```

Two things this corrected versus the first diagnosis. The loss is **16** significant digits, not 15 â€” PG 16 defaults `extra_float_digits` to 1, so `float8out` emits `%.17g`-minus-one rather than the shortest round-trip form. And roughly **27% of arbitrary doubles fail exact equality**, far more often than the observed ~1-in-3 _test_ failure rate implies; the test only trips when that run's particular computed confidence lands on a 17-digit value.

**Chosen: `toBeCloseTo(â€¦, 12)` for both fields** â€” tolerance 5e-13. That is >500x the worst measured error (8.9e-16) rather than fitted to it, since the error is fundamentally relative (~2 ULP) and the strictest passing precisions (15 for confidence, 14 for predictedKp) leave only 5-9x margin. It stays many orders of magnitude tighter than any semantically meaningful difference in a 0-1 confidence score or a 0-9 Kp index, so a genuine regression â€” wrong field, unit error, a rounding step applied on one side â€” is still caught. `accuracy.test.ts`'s `actualKp` assertions were checked and deliberately left on `.toBe()`: they compare against small exact integers (4, 5, 6, 7), which round-trip bit-for-bit.

Also added `expect(auroraCard!.confidence).not.toBeNull()` â€” needed for types once `confidence` (`number | null`) feeds `toBeCloseTo`, and independently meaningful, since an active CME is the precondition that makes the row persistable at all.

**Gates (real runs):** `vitest run --coverage` (apps/api) exit 0, 38 files / 394 tests; `brief.test.ts` run **8 consecutive times standing alone, 8/8 green** â€” against a pre-fix base rate of ~1 failure per 3 runs, so p â‰ˆ 0.04 of that happening by chance, and the measured 500x tolerance margin is the real evidence. `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/api/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Phase 8 â†’ Phase 9 transition, with three items explicitly accepted as open (human decision)

**Decision (the human's, not the agent's):** move to Phase 9 now, without waiting for tonight's real-satellite-rendering visual verification. `WORKPLAN.md`'s Current Phase marker is updated to `Phase 9`.

This is a **deliberate exception** to two of this repo's own rules â€” `WORKPLAN.md` Global Operating Rule 1 ("Order is lawâ€¦ do not start a phase until the previous phase's Definition of Done is fully met") and `CLAUDE.md`'s "Don't mark a phase done because 'most of it works' â€” the Definition of Done gate is binary." Logging it rather than quietly re-interpreting the gate, so that the next agent to read the marker does not mistake Phase 8 for verified-complete.

**Phase 8's stated Definition of Done:** _"A user drops from orbit into their real night sky, clicks the ISS and gets its story, and sees a live aurora ring â€” all at a stable framerate."_ The code for every clause exists and its math/logic is tested; what is unverified is the **on-screen** half.

**Carried forward as accepted open items â€” not dropped, not silently closed:**

1. **Real-satellite-rendering visual verification.** The full on-screen rendering of the real (non-simulated) satellite population has never been confirmed in a real browser. `/api/satellites` needs a running backend (docker-compose Postgres + `NASA_API_KEY` + `N2YO_API_KEY`), which no agent sandbox has had. The propagation math itself _is_ independently verified (matched Python `skyfield` to ~0.0002Â°/0.0008Â° alt/az on two real CelesTrak `visual`-group satellites), and `/explore` was confirmed to load cleanly headless â€” so the risk is integration/visual, not algorithmic. **Owner: the human, in a real browser with the backend up.**
2. **The Â§11 0:20 orbit-drop cinematic.** Currently implemented as a _ground-view_ cinematic lock (1.6s expo.inOut yaw/pitch/FOV tween onto the ISS, then per-frame follow). The literal "rise from ground to orbit, Earth resolves" flight needs an orbit-scale camera view that does not exist in the scene yet. Already documented as a deferral on 2026-07-27; restated here so it survives the phase boundary. **Owner: a deliberate design call â€” build the orbit view, or amend Â§11 to match the ground-view reading. Not a bug to be fixed in passing.**
3. **The diegetic-font / DevTools check.** `DiegeticText` renders via troika-three-text against a self-hosted `MartianMono-Regular.ttf` (troika parses font files itself and cannot use the DOM's Google-Fonts pipeline). That the TTF actually loads and glyphs render in-scene â€” rather than silently falling back â€” has only been reasoned about, never confirmed in DevTools against a running scene. **Owner: the human, same browser session as item 1.**

**What this does not change:** the three items above remain _open work_, and Phase 8 is recorded as **closed-with-exceptions**, not **done**. Anything Phase 9 builds must not assume they were resolved. Phase 9's own Definition of Done is unaffected and is still a binary gate.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Phase 9 backend: `/api/best-spot` candidate generation, scoring inputs, and four spec gaps

Backend half of the Best-Spot-Tonight Finder. Scoring itself is **entirely** `packages/shared`'s Â§11 engine â€” `clarityFromCloudFraction`, `darknessFromBortle`, `travelDecay`, `bestSpotScoreAurora`, `auroraVisibility`, `auroraStrengthToFactor` â€” with nothing re-derived in the API layer. What follows are the decisions the specs did not make for us.

**1. Â§11 defines `distance_km` but never says which metric produces it.** Added `packages/shared/src/geo.ts` (`haversineDistanceKm`, `destinationPoint`) on Â§0's already-frozen `R_EARTH_KM`. Deliberately _not_ a new FORMULAS.md section â€” this is standard spherical geodesy, not a physical model, and inventing a Â§13 for it would inflate a locked doc. Spherical rather than ellipsoidal: WGS84 would disagree by ~0.3%, far below the resolution of anything Â§11 consumes (a 50 km travel-decay scale scored against an ~11 km Bortle grid). Verified against an independent formula (spherical law of cosines) and published great-circle distances before use: NYCâ†’LA 3935.75 km, 1Â° longitude at the equator 111.1949 km, pole-to-pole = Ï€Â·R. **Flagged for the human:** if Â§11 should pin the metric explicitly, that is a one-line clarification, not a behaviour change.

**2. No candidate-generation strategy is specified anywhere.** Chose a deterministic polar grid: concentric rings at 10/25/40/55 km Ã— the 8 principal compass bearings, plus the observer's own position as the zero-travel baseline â€” 33 candidates. The envelope is derived from Â§11 itself rather than picked by feel: `travel = exp(-distance_km / 50)` has decayed to 0.33 by 55 km, so sites further out cannot rank no matter how dark they are. Deterministic by design, so a response is reproducible and diffable.

**3. DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§12's mockup shows a place name ("CHERRY VALLEY") and a drive time ("38 min"). Neither has a data source in ARCHITECTURE.md.** Its only mapping entry is MapLibre GL JS, a client-side renderer â€” there is no geocoder and no routing service anywhere in the locked stack. Per `CLAUDE.md` ("don't add a service not named in ARCHITECTURE.md without logging the decision first") neither was added, and â€” more importantly â€” neither was faked:

- **Names** are honest derived labels (`"NE 25 km"`, `"Your location"`). Inventing plausible place names for grid points would be fabricated data of exactly the kind this codebase has already had to strip out once (the Phase 8 satellite-magnitude removal).
- **`travelMinutes` is always `null`**, with the field present in the schema so that adding a routing source later is not a breaking change. Deriving minutes from an assumed average speed would be a fabricated measurement wearing a real unit.
  Closing either gap is a data-source decision for the human, not something to improvise.

**4. Â§11's aurora term needed an interpretation.** Read literally per-site it inverts the ranking: `aurora_factor âˆˆ [0,1]` multiplies in, so an aurora-visible site would score _lower_ than one outside the oval. Â§11's own wording ("rank by `score` **or** `score_aurora`, on aurora nights") makes it a per-night mode, not a per-site term. Implemented as an opt-in `?event=aurora` filter â€” WORKPLAN.md Phase 9's "filter by tonight's event". Within that mode a site that genuinely cannot see aurora scores 0, which is honest. But if _no_ candidate can see it, the ranking falls back to the plain score with an explanatory note rather than returning an all-zero list â€” that is precisely the failure Â§11 warns about ("would incorrectly zero out an otherwise-good site").

**5. Unavailable clarity is omitted from the product, never passed through as 0.** Same reasoning, applied to DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§12's cloud-outage clause: Â§11 is multiplicative and "any zero kills the site", so a missing cloud reading would blank every site if zeroed. Score becomes `darkness Ã— travel`, `ranking.basis` flips to `darkness-travel`, and the payload carries Â§12's reduced-confidence header note.

**6. A missing Bortle class drops the candidate entirely.** Darkness has no honest substitute in a _dark-sky_ ranking â€” a site scored on travel alone would rank on convenience while claiming to rank on darkness. If no candidate resolves, the endpoint returns `status: 'unavailable'` with an empty list, per ARCHITECTURE.md Â§5, rather than 500ing.

**7. Bortle lookup existed only inside a test file.** `light-pollution.client.test.ts` had a private `getBortle` helper; there was no production client at all. Added `clients/static/light-pollution.client.ts` (memoised, never-throws, same contract as the network clients). It reads the grid from `apps/web/public/data/bortle-grid.bin` rather than duplicating a 6.5 MB binary into `apps/api` â€” the frontend map layer serves the same file, and the relative path resolves identically from `src/` and compiled `dist/`. Out-of-scale cells are **rejected, not clamped**: `darknessFromBortle(0)` returns 1.125, so one corrupt cell would silently outrank every real site.

**8. Open-Meteo batching, verified live rather than assumed.** One request per candidate would multiply this endpoint's external API cost by 33. Confirmed against the real API that comma-separated coordinates return a JSON _array_ in request order, and added `fetchOpenMeteoBatch`. Two traps found in doing so, both now covered by tests: the API **snaps coordinates to its model grid and echoes the snapped value** (a request for 32.73 returns 32.724075), so results are keyed by index and the _requested_ coordinate is what the response reports â€” otherwise a site marker would sit up to ~5 km from the point actually scored; and a short or schema-invalid array degrades every point rather than being zipped against the candidate list, which would mis-attribute one site's cloud cover to another.

**9. Bug found and fixed in the existing Open-Meteo client.** It returns _naive_ ISO timestamps ("2026-07-27T00:00") with no zone designator even when the request sets `timezone=UTC` â€” `new Date()` parses those as **local** time, so on this machine every hour silently shifted by +5:30. Pinned to UTC at the client boundary (`toUtcIso`) so no consumer can inherit it. Pre-existing and latent only because nothing consumed `hourly.time` until now: Open-Meteo is not wired into the poller at all.

**10. `at` query param instead of an invented "tonight".** Cloud cover is read at the hour nearest `at`, defaulting to now. No doc defines which hour "Best-Spot-**Tonight**" should score, and hard-coding an observing-hour policy would bury a product decision in the backend. The caller passes the instant it means; `packages/shared`'s twilight engine can supply it later.

**11. Stale comment corrected in `packages/shared/src/engines/best-spot.ts`.** Its docstring claimed Â§11 "gives no normalization formula" for the aurora factor. Â§11 was amended on 2026-07-15 to specify exactly that (`AURORA_STRENGTH_SATURATION_DEG = 20`), and `auroraStrengthToFactor` has implemented it since. Comment now points at the real function instead of contradicting the spec it cites.

## 2026-07-27 â€” Phase 10 backend: what already existed, and the five judgement calls Phase 10 needed

Backend half of Log, Settings & Accuracy. The first finding was how much of this Phase 6 had already built, so the shape of the work was mostly _verify and compose_, not _create_.

**Already built â€” reused, not rebuilt:** `/api/locations` (full CRUD, per-user isolated, with `isDefault` promotion/demotion in transactions), `/api/sky-log` (CRUD), `DELETE /api/auth/account`, `runAccuracyJob`, `getGlobalPredictionHistory`, and `historyFactor`. Nothing here reimplements any of them. Three consequences worth stating plainly:

1. **The task asked for `DELETE /api/account`; the real route is `DELETE /api/auth/account`.** No alias was added â€” one destructive operation should have exactly one path, and a second route to the same cascade is a thing that later drifts. Flagged for the human rather than silently satisfied.
2. **`/api/settings` deliberately does not re-expose saved locations.** It reports the current _default_ read-only so Â§15's page can render it; create/update/delete stay at `/api/locations`. Two contracts over the same rows is the drift `CLAUDE.md` makes the lead agent responsible for preventing.
3. **The deletion cascade had never actually been tested.** The route deletes only the `User` row and relies entirely on `onDelete: Cascade` FKs â€” a _database_ behaviour, so a mocked Prisma would have proved nothing. `account-deletion.test.ts` now populates a session, location, sky-log entry and prediction, asserts all four exist (so the post-delete assertion can't pass vacuously), deletes, and asserts all four are zero. ARCHITECTURE.md Â§7's "deletion means deletion" is now a verified claim rather than a stated intention.

**1. Two locked docs disagree on the Log's headline stats.** DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§13 says "nights observed, ISS passes caught, last aurora"; ARCHITECTURE.md Â§8 and WORKPLAN.md Phase 10 both say "total, streak, last aurora". Rather than pick a winner, `/api/log` returns all five (`totalSightings`, `nightsObserved`, `issPassesCaught`, `lastAuroraSighting`, `currentStreakNights`) â€” every figure is real and cheap to derive, and the page renders whichever three Â§13 wants. **Flagged for the human:** one of the two docs should probably be amended so a future agent doesn't re-litigate this.

**2. The "observing night" boundary is 12:00 UTC.** Â§13's stat is _nights_, not calendar days, so an entry at 01:00 belongs to the night that began the previous evening â€” a UTC calendar date would count one session as two nights for most of Europe and Asia. Noon is not an arbitrary pick: it is the same convention FORMULAS.md Â§3's Julian Date already uses, precisely so a night carries one integer day number. **Limitation, stated rather than hidden:** it is UTC-anchored and therefore exact only near the prime meridian. A true per-observer boundary needs the observer's timezone, which `SCHEMA.md` does not store.

**3. A streak that ended last night still counts as current.** The night is not over until it is over, so a user who logged something last night but not yet tonight has broken nothing. A run whose most recent night is older than that reports 0 rather than presenting a stale historical streak as if it were live.

**4. DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§14's "no cherry-picking controls" is enforced in the API, not left to the UI.** `/api/accuracy` accepts **no query parameters at all** â€” no `from`, `to`, `since`, `range`, `order`, and deliberately **no `limit`**, since "most recent N" is exactly the selector Â§14 forbids. A client that wanted to show only its best week would have to fetch the whole record and discard part of it, which makes the omission a visible act in reviewable frontend code rather than an innocuous query string. This is asserted, not just documented: `accuracy.test.ts` seeds two January misses and one June hit, then checks that seven different filter attempts return byte-identical results. The response schema goes further and rejects any payload whose `series` is shorter than `hitRate.trials` â€” a truncated record with an intact trials count is the quiet version of the same cherry-pick.

**5. `/accuracy` is public, confirmed against two locked docs before deciding** â€” ARCHITECTURE.md Â§8's route table marks it `public`, and DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§14 opens with "Public." Consequently no point carries a `userId` or prediction id (the query `select`s only four columns, and the schema is `.strict()` so one cannot be added by accident). The rolling rate reuses `getGlobalPredictionHistory` and `historyFactor` unchanged: computing a second, differently-scoped rate here would let the public track record disagree with the confidence figure the Brief shows, which is the premise `predictions/history.ts` exists to protect. Note that `trials` is inherently public â€” Â§14 requires the Beta prior be explained â€” so prediction volume is disclosed by design, not by oversight.

**6. Alert toggles default to off, and the payload says they cannot be delivered.** No doc states a default, and ARCHITECTURE.md contains **no alert delivery mechanism at all** â€” no push service, no mail transport. Defaulting to on would record a consent the user never gave, for notifications nothing can send. The response carries `alertsDeliverable: false` so Â§15's ALERTS section can say so plainly rather than implying a working feature. **Flipping the default when delivery actually ships is a product decision for the human.** The blob is also merged, never replaced, on write: `SCHEMA.md` expects `User.settings` to accumulate preferences, so a write that dropped another feature's key would be silent cross-feature data loss.

**7. One event-type vocabulary, not two.** `/api/sky-log` had its closed enum inline; Phase 10's alerts cover the same four real events. Both now import `util/event-types.ts` (`ALERTABLE_EVENT_TYPES`, plus `other` for the log), so the frontend learns one set of names and the two cannot drift.

**8. `/api/brief` now falls back to the authenticated user's default location** â€” ARCHITECTURE.md Â§8 states "Logged-out = generic location; logged-in = saved location", and this is where that override lands now that Phase 6's `Location` rows have a settings surface. This is a change to a **Phase 4** endpoint, so: it is purely additive (explicit coordinates still win, anonymous callers with no coordinates still get the same 400, and all nine pre-existing brief tests pass untouched). One behaviour was tightened while writing the tests â€” a half-specified position (`?lat=65` with no `lon`) now 400s rather than silently discarding the coordinate the caller _did_ send, which the first implementation did.

**Known scaling consideration, flagged not fixed:** `/api/log` and `/api/accuracy` both return their full history unbounded. For `/api/accuracy` that is Â§14's explicit requirement and must not be "fixed" with a limit. For `/api/log` it is a genuine choice â€” the streak and nights-observed stats are only correct over the complete set â€” but a user with years of entries will eventually want pagination on the _timeline_ while the stats stay whole. That needs a shape decision (stats separate from a paged timeline), so it is logged rather than guessed at now.

## 2026-07-29 â€” Phase 11 backend: two defects the share-card tests surfaced

Writing the `src/share` test suite (WORKPLAN.md Phase 11) turned up two things that code review had not, both now fixed in place. Recording them because in each case the _comment_ was the thing that was wrong, which is the failure mode `CLAUDE.md` makes the lead agent responsible for catching.

**1. `inkPaletteFor` was picking the less legible ink across a band of real twilights.** It chose ink by comparing the surface's Rec. 709 luminance to a hand-set threshold of 140. That constant is not where the two candidate inks actually cross over: measured against Â§4.1's ramp, sky-900 and sky-100 contrast equally at luminance **118.874**, so every surface between there and 140 was being given the _worse_ of the two. At the worst point the primary ink fell to **2.99:1** where **3.97:1** was available â€” and the affected band is centred on civil twilight, i.e. sunset, the moment a share card is most likely to be generated.

Fixed by deleting the threshold rather than re-tuning it: `inkPaletteFor` now measures both candidates with a WCAG `contrastRatio` and takes the better. No integer threshold can be exactly right (the crossover is not a round number in any luminance space), so a constant would have re-introduced a narrower version of the same bug and would need re-tuning if Â§4.1's tokens ever move. "Always the more legible ink" is now true by construction and asserted over 300 sampled points of the continuous ramp.

**Residual gap, flagged for the human â€” not closed.** Choosing optimally is not the same as passing. The mid-greys of Â§4.1's ramp around twilight value ~0.67â€“0.78 top out at **~3.97:1** for primary ink against the surface. That clears the 3:1 large-text floor, which is what the `display-l` headline needs, but it is **below Part VI's 4.5:1** for the small mono eyebrow and the fact row. This is a property of the locked palette, not of the code: with only `sky-100` and `sky-900` available as ink, no selection strategy reaches 4.5:1 there. Closing it needs one of â€” a new mid-ramp ink token in Â§4.1, a scrim/plate behind the mono text that Â§17's composition does not currently describe, or an accepted narrowing of the surface ramp so it never sits in that band. All three are design decisions on locked docs, so none was taken here. `og-svg.test.ts` pins the current state both ways: AA is asserted everywhere _outside_ value 0.66â€“0.79, and the worst case inside it is held above 3.9 so the gap cannot silently widen.

**2. `font-metrics.ts`'s header claimed more than the code does.** It stated that its widths and resvg's "are required to agree". They agree exactly on per-glyph advances â€” all 15 glyph comparisons pass at a hundredth of a pixel â€” but not on whole strings, because **resvg applies each face's GPOS kern pairs and this parser does not**. Measured at ~0.6% on an ordinary headline ("To" alone pulls in 3.2px at 40px).

Left unimplemented deliberately, and the comment corrected to say so. Kern adjustments are essentially always negative, so the parser's error is one-directional: it over-measures, which means `wrapText` breaks a line marginally early and the right-aligned CTA sits marginally inside its margin. Both are the safe side. An under-measure would clip the single artifact most non-users of ASTRANET will ever see, so the bias is worth keeping; a GPOS reader is disproportionate to what correcting ~0.6% in the safe direction would buy. The test now asserts the true properties â€” exact agreement per glyph, exact agreement on an unkerned string, and a bounded one-directional over-measure on a real headline.

### 2026-07-29 addendum â€” the contrast gap above was understated, and the remedy is now determined

The entry above reported the residual gap as "~3.97:1 for primary ink ... below Part VI's 4.5:1 for the small mono eyebrow and the fact row." Measuring per _element role and size_ rather than on ink alone shows that was wrong in both directions, and the correction changes which remedy is viable:

- **Ink never fails its applicable floor.** The `display-l` headline (75px) and the `data-l` fact _values_ (40px) are both WCAG large text, floor 3:1, worst case 3.99 â€” passing across the whole ramp. The 3.97 figure was real but belonged to an element that was never in breach.
- **The breach is in `brass` and `muted`.** Brass carries the 15px micro text (eyebrow, fact labels, compass ticks, marker labels) and bottoms out at **1.49:1**, failing on **28.2%** of the ramp. The 20px muted CTA bottoms out at **1.52:1**, failing on **44.0%**. Roughly a third of all twilights, not the 3.4% band originally recorded.

**The remedy is no longer a free choice between three options.** The best worst-case contrast a two-token selection strategy can reach is `sqrt(contrast(lightToken, darkToken))` â€” the value at the crossover, where both candidates are equal. Measured: ink `sqrt(15.833) = 3.979`, brass `sqrt(3.210) = 1.792`, muted `sqrt(3.084) = 1.756`, against the `4.5Â² = 20.25` pair contrast a 4.5:1 guarantee requires. Brass and muted are hue tokens whose two variants sit ~3:1 apart, so **no threshold, no selection rule and no narrowing of Â§4.1's ramp can lift them near 4.5:1**.

That eliminates two of the three options previously listed. Narrowing the ramp would have to delete ~44% of it (the whole civil segment plus part of nautical) to satisfy muted â€” removing twilight from a twilight product and gutting Â§17's premise precisely at sunset. A new ink token cannot work either: reaching 20.25 requires essentially pure white _and_ pure black, and Â§4.1 forbids pure black in the 2D interface.

**Recommended: a backing plate behind the instrument text, amending Â§17's composition and leaving Â§4.1 untouched.** On a `sky-900` plate the existing tokens give brass-300 **8.67:1**, muted sky-400 **6.03:1**, ink sky-100 **15.83:1**; on `sky-800`, **7.63 / 5.31 / 13.93**. All clear 4.5:1 with headroom using tokens that already exist. It should back only the eyebrow strip and the fact row so the headline and horizon band stay on bare surface, and it should be unconditional â€” a plate appearing for only part of the ramp would make the card's identity flicker with time of day and read as a rendering bug.

**Runner-up, for the human's comparison:** render micro text in `ink` instead of `brass` and raise it from 15px to â‰¥24px so the 3:1 large-text floor applies (ink's 3.99 then passes everywhere). No Â§4.1 change and no new element, but it costs Â§4.2's brass-instrument voice on every label and unbalances Â§17's hierarchy with chunky "micro" text. The obvious cheaper variant â€” enlarging the text while keeping brass â€” is dead, since brass's 1.79 ceiling is below even the 3:1 floor.

Still not taken here: all of these amend a locked doc, so the decision remains the human's. `og-svg.test.ts` continues to pin the current behaviour so it cannot regress in the meantime â€” but note those assertions cover `ink`, and therefore did **not** and do not encode the brass/muted breach described above.

### 2026-07-29 â€” resolved: Â§17 instrument plate implemented

The human approved the backing-plate remedy. DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§17 now specifies it (Â§4.1's ramp is untouched, as recommended): the eyebrow strip and the mono measurement row sit on a solid `sky-900` plate with `sky-100` ink and `brass-300` labels; the headline and Horizon Band stay on bare surface.

Implementation notes worth keeping:

- **The plate palette is a constant, not surface-derived.** `PLATE_PALETTE` in `og-svg.ts` is always the dark-surface token set. Deriving it from the surface would be the bug the plate exists to prevent: on a day surface `inkPaletteFor` returns `brass-700`, which against a `sky-900` plate is 1.6:1 â€” worse than the problem being fixed. Asserted directly in `og-svg.test.ts`.
- **The eyebrow plate hugs its text; the fact plate spans the content width.** The eyebrow is one variable-length run, so a fitted plate occludes the least surface â€” the twilight color is the point of the card. The fact row's three columns are already distributed across the full content width, so a spanning plate is the honest shape for it.
- **Unconditional, per Â§17.** Not applied only at the twilights that need it: a plate appearing and vanishing with solar altitude would make the card's identity flicker with time of day and read as a rendering fault.
- **Geometry verified against real font metrics, not estimated.** Both rects contain the ascender and descender of the largest face they back, with 41.8px clearance to the headline, 20.3px to the compass tick labels, and 5.0px to the footer baseline.

**The known exception is now recorded in Â§17 itself rather than only here:** the Horizon Band's compass ticks and marker labels (brass, micro) and the footer CTA (muted, body) remain on bare surface and stay below 4.5:1 across part of the ramp â€” worst 1.49:1 and 1.52:1 respectively. Backing them would either occlude the Band, which is the signature element, or add a second plate to the footer; Â§17 explicitly declines to make that composition call for now. `og-svg.test.ts` pins both worst-case figures so the gap stays visible and cannot be mistaken for solved.

Also corrected while here: `og-svg.ts`'s `inkPaletteFor` docstring still described the pre-plate residual gap as covering "the mono eyebrow and fact row". Those elements no longer sit on the surface, so the comment named the wrong consumers; it now points at `PLATE_PALETTE` for the arithmetic instead.

## 2026-07-29 â€” Phase 12 resilience + rate-limit verification: three findings, none fixed

Phase 12's own rule is hardening and measuring, not building, so everything below is **logged rather than fixed**. Two of the three are genuine defects; the third is a documentation error that would misinform the next capacity decision.

### 1. The degradation contract does not hold for space weather â€” the card reports `ok` during a total SWPC outage

Found by injecting failure at the **transport boundary** (`fetch`) and driving the real clients, the real poller tick, the real store and the real Express route â€” `routes/outage.test.ts`. Every prior resilience test injects at a seam one layer up, and this defect lives precisely in the gap between those seams.

`build-brief.ts` gates the card on store-entry nullness:

```ts
const spaceWeatherHasAnySource =
  pollerState.solarWind.data !== null || pollerState.spaceWeatherForecast.data !== null;
```

But **the poller never writes `data: null` for SWPC.** `fast-tier.ts`'s `writeSolarWindResult` on total failure with no prior value writes the failed result _as an object_ â€” `{ kpCurrent: null, rtswPlasma: null, fetchedAt }` â€” marked `healthy: false`. The slow tier does the same. So the gate can never open, and a complete SWPC outage yields `spaceWeather.status: 'ok'`.

ARCHITECTURE.md Â§5 states "Per-source health flags from the poller drive which cards degrade". **The `healthy` flag is not consulted at the card level at all** â€” only `data !== null` is.

**Why the existing test suite did not catch it:** `build-brief.test.ts`'s "SWPC entirely down blanks only the space-weather card" sets `state.solarWind = empty()` (`data: null`) â€” a state the real poller cannot produce for this source. The test proves the contract for an unreachable input.

**User-visible impact, traced to the frontend.** The card body is honest (`solarLine.headline` is literally `"space weather unavailable"`, all values null, nested `healthy: false`, `aurora: null`), so no fabricated data is shown. The _status_ is what lies, and three UI affordances key off it:

- `BriefPage.tsx:326` â€” `<LivePulse active={brief?.spaceWeather.status === 'ok'} />` renders the **live pulse as active** for a source that is entirely down. DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§4.3 restricts ember to live state; Â§7.2 defines the Live Pulse as exactly that signal.
- `BriefPage.tsx:319` / `:328` â€” the `opacity-50` dimming and the "unavailable" notice are both suppressed.
- `HorizonBand.tsx:180` â€” the "AURORA Â· FORECAST UNAVAILABLE" note is suppressed.

So the screen shows a live indicator next to a card reading "space weather unavailable". That is the "reads as though space weather was simply quiet" failure `routes/share.ts` already warns about, on the main page.

**Isolation itself is intact** â€” no other card is affected, and the Brief still renders â€” so this is a status-signalling defect, not a cascade. Every other source degrades correctly under all five injected modes (5xx, 503, 4xx, malformed, abort).

Pinned in `outage.test.ts` as two `KNOWN GAP:` tests asserting the _current_ behaviour, so a fix will fail them loudly and deliberately rather than silently.

### 2. N2YO's real limit for the endpoint that scales with users is 100/hr, not 1000/hr

`API_SOURCES.md` records "**Rate limit:** **1000 transactions/hour**" as a single global figure. N2YO's published documentation states "The REST API v1 is free but it is transaction limited **by type**", with per-endpoint limits:

| Endpoint         | Limit/hr |
| ---------------- | -------- |
| tle              | 1000     |
| positions        | 1000     |
| **visualpasses** | **100**  |
| radiopasses      | 100      |
| above            | 100      |

Verified against n2yo.com/api twice. Two consequences compound:

**(a) The wrong endpoint was budgeted.** The polled endpoint is `positions` (limit 1000). The endpoint carrying real user load is `visualpasses` â€” limit **100/hr** â€” and it was never in the budget at all.

**(b) `visualpasses` is not polled and not cached, so it scales with users.** `API_SOURCES.md` opens by asserting "Polling is **central** â€” one poller hits each source and fans results to all users... Upstream load is constant regardless of user count, which is what keeps every limit below comfortably satisfied." That premise is **false for `visualpasses`**: it is observer-specific (it needs lat/lon), so it cannot be centrally polled. `composeBriefForObserver` calls it live on **every** `GET /api/brief` and **every** `POST /api/share`, through no cache.

Measured upstream usage per hour (counted empirically by stubbing `fetch` and running both real tiers, not derived by reading code):

| Source       | Endpoint                       | Calls/hr             | Real limit              | Verdict        |
| ------------ | ------------------------------ | -------------------- | ----------------------- | -------------- |
| N2YO         | positions (fast tier)          | 80                   | 1000/hr                 | 8% â€” fine      |
| **N2YO**     | **visualpasses (per request)** | **1 per Brief view** | **100/hr**              | âš ï¸ **binding** |
| SWPC         | 5 endpoints (both tiers)       | 178                  | keyless, none published | fine           |
| NASA         | DONKI Ã—2 + NeoWs               | 18                   | ~1000/hr                | 1.8% â€” fine    |
| JPL Horizons | Sun + 5 planets                | 36                   | none published          | fine           |
| CelesTrak    | gp.php                         | 6                    | none published          | fine           |
| Open-Meteo   | 1 batch per Best-Spot request  | per request          | 600/min, 5k/hr, 10k/day | fine           |

**The ceiling is roughly 100 Brief page-loads per hour â€” about 1.7 per minute.** Not 1000. Aggravating factors:

- `/` **and** `/explore` both call `fetchBrief` on mount, so one user visiting both spends 2 of the 100. `BriefPage`'s effect also refires on location change.
- `POST /api/share` spends one more, and still has no rate limiting (deferred from Phase 11).
- **Retry amplification:** `fetchWithRetry` is 3 attempts with 500ms/1000ms backoff on 5xx and network errors (4xx correctly does not retry â€” verified in `outage.test.ts`). A flaky N2YO therefore consumes the 100/hr budget in as few as ~33 real page views.

The obvious mitigation is already half-built and unused: `cache/store.ts` is a Postgres-backed TTL cache, swept on a timer, whose own header says "Nothing user-facing consumes this directly yet". Visual passes are a good fit â€” they change slowly and are coarse in observer position. Wiring it is **new work, so not done here**; it belongs in a phase that owns the caching/rate-limit policy rather than being improvised during a hardening pass.

`API_SOURCES.md` has been corrected on this point (the 1000/hr figure and the rate-budget table), because leaving a known-wrong limit in the reference doc would misinform the next capacity decision. That is a factual correction to a non-locked doc, called out here rather than made silently.

### 3. Not a defect, recorded for completeness: `fetchWithRetry` is duplicated six times

`celestrak`, `jpl-horizons`, `n2yo`, `nasa`, `open-meteo` and `swpc` each carry their own copy. Compared line by line they are behaviourally identical â€” 10s timeout, 3 attempts, 500ms initial backoff doubling, 4xx not retried â€” with one deliberate difference: CelesTrak's takes a `parseResponse` parameter because its TLE endpoint returns plain text. So this is **not** an inconsistent-resilience finding; every source genuinely behaves the same under failure, which the outage suite confirms across all five modes.

It is still six copies of the same retry policy, so any future change to timeout or backoff has six places to miss. Consolidating into a shared module is the durable fix and is left for a phase that is allowed to add code.

### 2026-07-29 addendum â€” the space-weather degradation gap is fixed

Finding 1 above is closed. Two notes on how, because the obvious fix would have introduced a different bug.

**The gate is content-based, not `healthy`-based.** ARCHITECTURE.md Â§5 names the health flag as what "drives which cards degrade", and the instinct is to write `solarWind.healthy || spaceWeatherForecast.healthy`. That regresses a documented behaviour: API_SOURCES.md's SWPC fallback is "use last cached value with an aged freshness stamp; **if never fetched this session**, shows unavailable". A preserved reading is unhealthy but real, and the poller goes out of its way to keep it â€” gating on `healthy` would blank the card the moment SWPC wavered and throw that value away.

The four reachable states make the requirement clear:

| SWPC state        | store `data`                | `healthy` | card should be                  |
| ----------------- | --------------------------- | --------- | ------------------------------- |
| never fetched     | null                        | false     | unavailable                     |
| fetched OK        | values                      | true      | ok, live                        |
| failed, no prior  | **object with null fields** | false     | **unavailable** â† was `ok`      |
| failed, had prior | prior values                | false     | **ok, not live** â† must survive |

Only a content check satisfies all four, and it is what the ISS and NEO cards already do. `healthy` remains the _freshness_ signal (Â§6), travelling to the client on `solarLine.live/forecast` â€” which is precisely where the UI fix consumes it.

**Audit result for "does this pattern exist elsewhere": no.** `build-brief.ts` had the only card gate deciding availability from store-entry nullness. `iss` and `neoImagery` both gate on card content. The poller's own `previous.data !== null` checks (fast-tier Ã—1, slow-tier Ã—5) ask a different question â€” "is there a prior value worth preserving" â€” and are correct as written.

**Why it survived a full test suite.** `build-brief.test.ts` set `state.solarWind = empty()` (`data: null`), a state the poller only produces before the very first tick. The test proved the contract for an input the system cannot reach in production. Both reachable states are now covered.

**The UI carried a second, independent defect.** The API response was honest throughout â€” headline "space weather unavailable", every value null, nested `healthy: false`. The screen was not: `BriefPage` derived the Live Pulse from `status === 'ok'` alone, and the status notice hardcoded `LAST SEEN 12:00` irrespective of reality. Fixing the API gate alone would have fixed the dimming and the notice by accident while leaving the pulse wrong for the stale case, which the API cannot express through `status` at all. So the UI now models three states rather than two â€” live / stale / unavailable â€” in `lib/space-weather-status.ts`, tested directly rather than through the payload.

Both defects shipped for the same structural reason: the logic lived where the test config could not reach it (`build-brief`'s gate was only exercised through an unreachable fixture; the UI logic was inside a `.tsx`, and vitest here collects only `src/**/*.test.ts`).

**Still open, unrelated and out of scope:** the causal chain rendered directly beneath this section is hardcoded placeholder text â€” "FLARE 19 JUL", "CME ARRIVING 23 JUL Â±6h", "AURORA POSSIBLE AT YOUR LATITUDE" â€” with no binding to `brief.spaceWeather.data`. That is Phase 7/10 frontend work, not a Phase 12 resilience concern.

### 2026-07-29 addendum â€” finding 2 closed: visual passes now cached

The N2YO `visualpasses` rate-limit finding is resolved. `brief/visual-passes-cache.ts` puts the call behind the `Cache` table on a 5-minute TTL, wired in `compose-brief.ts` so both call sites (`GET /api/brief`, `POST /api/share`) are covered by one change.

**TTL: 5 minutes.** No locked doc pins one, so it was chosen against the two real constraints. Upstream: a single key costs at most `3600/300 = 12` calls/hour, leaving room for ~8 distinct observer positions inside the 100/hr this endpoint allows. Staleness: pass predictions derive from TLEs that N2YO and CelesTrak refresh once or twice a day, so five minutes of age sits far below the resolution of the source data. Crucially the _"next"_ pass is not what is cached â€” `iss-card.ts`'s `selectNextPass` re-filters the cached list against each request's own clock, so a cached payload cannot leave an already-started pass sitting at the top of the Brief.

**Key: the exact observer position, not a rounded grid.** Quantising to a grid would raise the hit rate for scattered coordinates, but it would serve passes computed for a point the observer is not standing at while presenting them as theirs â€” the same class of thing this codebase has refused three times already this phase. It is also unnecessary, which is the more interesting half: `getEffectiveLocation()` returns either a saved location (stable across reloads and across `/` and `/explore`) or `DEFAULT_OBSERVER_LOCATION`, which **every logged-out visitor shares**. Real traffic already collapses onto a handful of keys, and the anonymous majority onto exactly one. Coordinates are formatted to 4 decimal places (~11 m) purely so `51.5` and `51.5000` cannot produce two rows for one position â€” normalising the representation, not approximating the position.

**Failures are cached at the same TTL.** The instinct is to cache only successes, but an outage is precisely when the budget is most at risk: `fetchWithRetry` makes three attempts on 5xx and network errors, so an uncached failure path costs 3 upstream calls per page view and would exhaust 100/hr in ~33 views. Caching the failure bounds a total outage to 3 calls per TTL per key. The cost is that the ISS card stays unavailable for up to one TTL after N2YO recovers â€” acceptable, and in line with the poller's own 45sâ€“10min cadences.

**The cache cannot break the Brief.** A read or write that throws is logged and stepped over, leaving the live fetch as the answer; an optimisation that can take down the page it optimises is a liability. Verified against a Prisma pointed at an unreachable host: it fails fast with `PrismaClientInitializationError` and falls through, and the 17 brief-route tests still pass in 714 ms.

**Re-measured budget** (same method as before â€” stub `fetch`, drive the real code):

|                                  | before             | after                                            |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------ | ------------------------------------------------ |
| 600 anonymous page views in 1 hr | 600 calls          | **12 calls**                                     |
| cost model                       | per page view      | **per observer position**                        |
| ceiling                          | ~100 page-loads/hr | ~**8 positions** refreshing continuously (96/hr) |

`API_SOURCES.md`'s table, its per-source entry and its "polling is central" binding rule are all updated to match.

**What is now the tightest case, stated plainly:** a _total_ N2YO outage. Retries make each refresh cost 3 calls, so 36/hr per key, and only ~2 distinct positions fit inside 100/hr. That is far better than the ~33 page views it used to take, but it is the constraint that would bite first, and raising the TTL is the lever. Not tuned further now because the right value depends on real traffic nobody has yet.

**Unchanged and still open:** `POST /api/share` remains an unauthenticated write with no rate limiting of its own. The cache bounds its _upstream_ cost, but not the number of rows an anonymous caller can create.

## 2026-07-30 â€” Major scope decision: authentication removed entirely

**Why:** Explicit human decision. No login/signup, no accounts, no per-user data anywhere in the product. Location becomes a client-side setting anyone can change without an account (default changed from Jammu to Delhi â€” see below). Sky Log and Settings become local-only (browser storage), not server-persisted per-account. Executed in two stages per the human's explicit process: a Stage 1 audit-and-plan pass with no code changes, presented and approved, then Stage 2 execution â€” precisely because a prior, unlogged attempt at this same task had skipped the audit, hand-edited `schema.prisma` without a real migration, and left the build broken (13 `tsc` errors); that attempt was discarded entirely and none of it survived into this one.

**Data-loss check before touching anything:** queried the running dev Postgres directly (read-only `SELECT COUNT(*)`) before any schema change. `User`, `Session`, `Location`, `SkyLogEntry`, `Prediction`, `ShareCard` were all confirmed at 0 rows. Nothing was lost.

**Schema â€” real migration, not a hand-edit.** `prisma/schema.prisma`: removed `User`, `Session`, `Location`, `SkyLogEntry` entirely; dropped `Prediction.userId` and `ShareCard.userId` (and their indexes/FKs). Applied via `npx prisma migrate dev --name remove_auth` against the real dev database (migration `20260730074145_remove_auth`) â€” never edited by hand without a generated migration. `SCHEMA.md` rewritten to match: three models remain (`Prediction`, `ShareCard`, `Cache`), all owner-less.

**`Prediction` was already logically global before this (2026-07-22 entry above) â€” this just removed the now-meaningless FK.** The row-level `userId` only ever recorded "whose Brief triggered this" for delete-my-data cascade, which no longer exists. Real behavior change, flagged rather than silently decided: `routes/brief.ts` previously only wrote a `Prediction` row when the caller was authenticated **and** an active CME was present; it now writes on **every** qualifying request regardless of caller, since there is no "caller identity" left to gate on. This means more DB writes than before, and is what keeps `/api/accuracy`'s global track record growing rather than starving it going forward â€” human confirmed this reasoning explicitly during Stage 1 approval.

**`ShareCard.userId` dropped too** â€” it was nullable and existed only so delete-my-data could cascade-remove a user's shared cards. Every share was already effectively anonymous-first; now every share simply has no owner, full stop.

**Backend deleted wholesale:** `auth/*` (require-auth, jwt, password, refresh-token, refresh-cookie, oauth-state-cookie + tests), `clients/google/*` (OAuth client + tests), `routes/auth.ts`, `routes/locations.ts`, `routes/sky-log.ts`, `routes/log.ts` (+ `log/build-log.ts`, `log/log.schemas.ts` â€” this composed view duplicated logic `apps/web/src/lib/phase10-helpers.ts` already had client-side), `routes/settings.ts` (+ `settings/user-settings.ts`, `settings/settings.schemas.ts`), `routes/account-deletion.test.ts`. `app.ts`/`index.ts` stripped of the corresponding route registrations and `jwtAccessSecret`/Google env wiring. `routes/brief.ts` lost `tryAuthenticate` and the saved-location fallback branch â€” the latter was already dead code in practice, since the frontend has always sent explicit `lat`/`lon` via `getEffectiveLocation()`. `routes/share.ts`/`share/store.ts` lost `tryAuthenticate` and the `userId` param. Test files that fixture-created `User`/`Location` rows or passed `jwtAccessSecret` (`brief.test.ts`, `share.test.ts`, `accuracy.test.ts`, `share/store.test.ts`, `db/client.test.ts`, `predictions/{history,accuracy}.test.ts`, plus one-line `jwtAccessSecret` drops in `health.test.ts`, `satellites.test.ts`, `best-spot.test.ts`, `stream.test.ts`, `outage.test.ts`, `phase3-orchestration.test.ts`) were updated to track their own created-row ids for cleanup instead of scoping by a test-user email suffix, since there's no user row to scope by anymore. `db/client.test.ts`'s generic "does the shared Prisma client work" round-trip switched from `User`/`Session` (gone) to `Cache`. Dependencies `argon2`, `jose`, `cookie`, `@types/cookie` removed from `apps/api/package.json` (confirmed unused via repo-wide grep first) and `npm install` re-run clean (8 packages removed).

**Frontend:** `pages/LoginPage.tsx` deleted; `App.tsx` lost the `/login`/`/signup` routes and the `user`-gated redirects on `/log`/`/settings` (both now always render). `store/index.ts` dropped `AuthUser`/`user`/`setUser`/`clearUser` and gained `zustand/middleware`'s `persist` (localStorage, key `astranet-store`) so `location`, new `skyLogEntries`, new `alerts`, and `redLightMode` all survive a reload â€” `navVisible` is deliberately excluded from persistence (session-only UI state; persisting it would let a reload restore a stale auto-hidden nav). `lib/api.ts` lost every server CRUD function for locations/settings/sky-log/account (`fetchLocations`, `createSavedLocation`, `setDefaultLocation`, `deleteSavedLocation`, `fetchSettings`, `updateAlertSettings`, `fetchSkyLog`, `createSkyLogEntry`, `deleteSkyLogEntry`, `deleteAccount`) and the now-meaningless `credentials: 'include'` on every remaining fetch (nothing sets a cookie anymore). `LogPage.tsx` and `SettingsPage.tsx` rewritten against the store instead of the deleted routes.

**Three scope decisions made explicit and confirmed by the human during Stage 1, not decided silently:**

1. **Saved-locations list collapsed to a single current-location setting** â€” `SettingsPage`'s old multi-location CRUD (list + one default) is gone; there's one `store.location`, editable inline in `/settings` or via the Brief page's location switcher, applying everywhere `getEffectiveLocation()` is read (`BriefPage`, `ExplorePage`, `BestSpotPage` â€” unchanged call sites, since the store was already the single shared source of truth for all three).
2. **"DELETE MY ACCOUNT & DATA" replaced with "Clear local data"** â€” wipes `location`, `skyLogEntries`, and `alerts` from this browser's `localStorage` (`store.clearLocalData()`). There is no server call; there is nothing server-side to delete.
3. **Prediction persistence is now unconditional** â€” see above.

**BriefPage's "CHANGE LOCATION" control was a stub before this task** (`onClick={() => alert(...)}`) â€” it never actually worked, auth or not. Building the real inline editor (label/lat/lon, writing to the shared store) was this task's natural place to finally implement it, since the whole point of the client-side-location requirement is that this control has to actually work.

**Default location changed: Jammu (32.73Â°N, 74.87Â°E) â†’ Delhi (28.6139Â°N, 77.2090Â°E),** same rigor as the 2026-07-27 Srinagarâ†’Jammu change: `DESIGN_SPEC.md`'s two locked example lines (Â§10 eyebrow strip, Â§11 opening sequence) updated, and the eyebrow's civil-twilight-end time recomputed genuinely rather than copied â€” ran the real `sunAltitudeDeg` engine (`packages/shared/src/engines/sun-position.ts`, compiled `dist`, same Meeus transform `FORMULAS.md` Â§3/Â§4 specifies) via a bisection scan for Delhi's âˆ’6Â° crossing on Wed 22 Jul 2026: **19:42 IST** (UTC crossing `2026-07-22T14:12:04.656Z`), replacing the stale copied-over `20:01`.

**Found during gate verification, fixed:** `apps/api/src/util/event-types.ts` was left orphaned once `routes/sky-log.ts` and `routes/settings.ts` (its only two consumers) were deleted â€” confirmed via a repo-wide grep with zero remaining references, then deleted. `apps/api/vitest.config.ts`'s coverage `include`/`exclude` still listed `src/log/**`, `src/settings/**`, `src/auth/**` (harmless as dead glob patterns, but stale); trimmed. Real gates, all green after: `tsc --build --force` (root, all three workspaces) exit 0; `eslint` (root + both apps) exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on every file this task touched; `apps/api` â€” 44 files/625 tests passing, 98.52% line coverage; `apps/web` â€” 9 files/120 tests passing (no coverage threshold configured there, by pre-existing design â€” component code is verified live-browser, not jsdom); `packages/shared` (untouched, sanity-checked) â€” 15 files/188 tests passing.

**Locked docs updated, not just logged here, per this file's own standing rule:** `ARCHITECTURE.md` (Â§1 diagram â€” the `AUTH (JWT)`/`+ Argon2` box relabeled `DB (Prisma)`/`Predictions`, matching real widths character-for-character so the box-drawing stays aligned; Â§2 backend stack table's Auth row removed; Â§3.A/Â§3.B reworded; Â§3 G marked retired rather than renumbered, same numbering-stability precedent as the 2026-07-27 Moon-section entry; Â§7 rewritten around "no personal data on the server to protect"; Â§8's route table dropped the Auth column and the `/login`,`/signup` row, `/log` and `/settings` now read "public"; Â§10 non-goals line strengthened to "no account system at all"). `SCHEMA.md` fully rewritten (three owner-less models, `User`/`Session`/`Location`/`SkyLogEntry` sections removed, `ShareCard` documented for the first time â€” it existed in the schema since Phase 11 but was never added to this doc, an unrelated pre-existing gap fixed while already rewriting the file for accuracy). `DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§10/Â§11 (Delhi lines above), Â§13 ("Auth required" â†’ public/local-only), Â§15 (section labels `SAVED LOCATIONS`/`ACCOUNT` â†’ `LOCATION`, the your-data paragraph rewritten around clear-local-data), Â§16 marked "removed" rather than deleted/renumbered so `Â§17`'s existing citations elsewhere stay valid. `WORKPLAN.md` gained a callout (same style as the existing Phase 8 exception note) at the top flagging that Phases 5/6/10's task lists describe what was originally built, not the current system â€” left as historical record rather than rewritten, matching this project's standing practice of not retroactively rewriting closed phases.

## 2026-07-30 â€” Personal Sky Log (`/log`) removed from the frontend entirely

**Why:** Explicit human decision, same day as the auth removal above.
