Purely a frontend change: the backend `SkyLogEntry` table, `/api/sky-log`, and `/api/log` routes were already gone (deleted in the auth-removal work above), so there was no schema or migration involved this time.

**Deleted:** `apps/web/src/pages/LogPage.tsx`. `App.tsx` lost the `/log` route (and the route-table doc comment's mention of it). `PersistentNav.tsx` lost the "Log" nav link. `store/index.ts` lost the `SkyLogEntryData` type and the entire `skyLogEntries`/`addSkyLogEntry`/`removeSkyLogEntry` slice, including from `clearLocalData()` and the `persist` middleware's `partialize`.

**`lib/phase10-helpers.ts` checked before touching, not assumed:** a repo-wide grep confirmed `calculateLogStats`, `groupEntriesByMonth`, and `formatEventTypeLabel` had no callers left besides `LogPage.tsx` and their own tests â€” deleted. `generateStepPlotPaths` is still used by `AccuracyPage.tsx` â€” kept, along with its tests. The file (and its test file) are not renamed despite the now-inaccurate "phase10" name â€” out of scope for a feature-removal task, and renaming would ripple into an import-path change with no functional benefit.

**`SettingsPage.tsx`'s "Clear local data" copy updated** (both the section description and the confirmation-modal text) to drop its "Sky Log entries" mention, matching what the action actually wipes now (location + alerts only).

**Full-repo grep for dead references, not just the obvious files:** searched `apps/web/src` for every spelling (`LogPage`, `skyLog`, `sky-log`, `sky log`, `/log`, `LOG SIGHTING`, the helper function names) plus a second broader pass (`journal`, `witnessed`, `streak`, `nights observed`) in case `BriefPage`/`ExplorePage` linked to it contextually without the literal word "log" â€” neither did. Zero remaining references after the edits above.

**Docs:** `DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§13 marked "removed" rather than deleted outright, same precedent as Â§16's earlier removal (section numbers stay stable for existing citations to `Â§14` onward) â€” the section's old "Empty state" paragraph (leftover, describing copy that no longer exists) was removed with it. The Part VII voice-guide table had a "Do" example that was literally `/log`'s old empty-state copy verbatim; swapped for a Best-Spot degradation example instead, since a voice guideline citing a deleted feature's exact wording would read as describing something that still exists. `ARCHITECTURE.md` â€” the Â§1 diagram's nav-item line lost ", Log" (same character-width-matching discipline as the earlier `AUTH`â†’`DB (Prisma)` box edit), Â§7's local-data bullets and Â§8's nav description/route table lost their Sky Log mentions, and the `/log` row was dropped from Â§8's table entirely. `SCHEMA.md` â€” both places describing Location/Sky Log/alerts as "client-side state" corrected to say Sky Log was removed outright, not relocated. `WORKPLAN.md` â€” the 2026-07-30 auth-removal callout amended (it had said Sky Log was "now client-side state," no longer true) plus a new one-line callout for this specific removal.

**Gates, real output:** `tsc --build --force` (root) exit 0; `eslint . --max-warnings=0` (root) exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on every touched file; `vitest run` (`apps/web`) â€” all tests passing post-removal (`phase10-helpers.test.ts` trimmed to just its surviving `generateStepPlotPaths` coverage, not left asserting on deleted functions).

## 2026-07-30 â€” `apps/api`'s `f_hist` flaky test fixed: dedicated serialized Vitest project, not a loosened assertion

**Why:** NOTES.md (2026-07-29) named the flake and three possible fixes without picking one. Asked explicitly to choose between serializing the DB-touching test files and loosening the racing assertion â€” loosening was rejected as the worse fix: it doesn't touch the actual bug (a real race on shared Postgres), it just makes the test tolerant of wrong answers, trading away the precision `predictions/history.test.ts` and `routes/brief.test.ts`'s `f_hist` case are specifically there to check. Serialization fixes the actual cause.

**Reproduced before touching anything, and the blast radius is bigger than NOTES.md's single named test.** Two consecutive full-suite runs on unmodified `main`: run 1 failed in `routes/accuracy.test.ts`, run 2 failed 3 tests across `routes/brief.test.ts`. A repo-wide grep for `prisma.prediction.` confirmed **four** test files write to the global `Prediction` table and assert an exact global count/value derived from it: `routes/brief.test.ts` (the `f_hist` case), `routes/accuracy.test.ts` (before/after deltas â€” a concurrent write landing inside a test's own before/after window corrupts the delta the same way), `predictions/accuracy.test.ts` (`closes the loop` asserts an exact global `{hits, trials}`), and `predictions/history.test.ts` (all three cases, including one asserting the table is globally empty). NOTES.md's "six test files" figure was the count of DB-touching files generally; the actual racing set for _this_ bug is these four specifically â€” every other DB-touching file (`share/store.test.ts`, `routes/share.test.ts`, `db/client.test.ts`, `cache/store.test.ts`, `brief/visual-passes-cache.test.ts`) touches a different table (`ShareCard`, `Cache`) or only asserts a type/shape, not an exact value, so serializing them too would have been unnecessary cost.

**Fix: `apps/api/vitest.workspace.ts`, a two-project split** â€” a `db-serial` project containing exactly those four files, a `unit` project with everything else. Only `db-serial` pays the serialization cost; the other 40 files stay fully parallel, so full-suite wall time is unchanged (~91-98s before and after, measured directly, not assumed).

**Two real implementation bugs found and fixed while building this, neither a guess:**

1. First attempt used `extends: './vitest.config.ts'` plus a per-project `include` override, on the assumption the override would replace the base config's `include: ['src/**/*.test.ts']`. It didn't â€” verified directly with `vitest run --project=db-serial --reporter=verbose`, which showed the "restricted" project still collecting all 44 files (`font-metrics.test.ts`, `slow-tier.test.ts`, etc.), producing a suite that reported 84 files / 1205 tests (roughly double) and _new_ failures in `visual-passes-cache.test.ts`/`db/client.test.ts` from the same file now running twice concurrently. Fixed by dropping `extends` and restating `include`/`exclude` fully inline in both projects, removing any config-merge ambiguity.
2. First attempt at the serialization key itself used `fileParallelism: false`, following NOTES.md's own wording. Verified this was silently doing nothing: `grep -rl fileParallelism node_modules/vitest node_modules/@vitest` â€” zero matches. That option doesn't exist in this project's pinned `vitest@2.1.9` at all (it's a later-version option); setting it is a no-op, which is exactly why the four files kept racing even restricted to their own project. The real 2.1.9 mechanism, found by grepping vitest's own compiled source for `poolOptions`: `pool: 'forks'`, `poolOptions.forks.singleFork: true`.

**A `--max-warnings=0` eslint side effect:** `vitest.workspace.ts` sits at the `apps/api` package root, outside `apps/api/tsconfig.json`'s `include: ["src", ...]`, so type-aware linting failed with "none of those TSConfigs include this file." The sibling `vitest.config.ts` never hit this because the root `.eslintrc.json`'s `ignorePatterns` already exempts `*.config.ts` â€” `vitest.workspace.ts` doesn't match that glob. Added `"vitest.workspace.ts"` to `ignorePatterns` explicitly rather than restructuring the tsconfig `include`, matching the existing precedent for vitest's own config files.

**Stale DB state found while verifying, unrelated to the fix itself but worth recording:** the two pre-fix reproduction runs (plus one aborted by a 5-minute command timeout mid-run) left 35 orphaned `Prediction` rows in the local dev Postgres â€” test rows whose `afterAll` cleanup never ran because the process didn't reach it. Confirmed via a direct `SELECT COUNT(*)` before assuming the fix worked; a subsequent single-run pass on that polluted DB still failed once, which would have wrongly looked like the fix not working had the pollution not been checked and cleared (`DELETE FROM "Prediction"`) first. This is a real, previously-undocumented failure mode of this test suite's design (a crashed run's DB-side leftovers can taint the next run) â€” not fixed here since it's outside this task's scope, but flagging it: anyone chasing a DB-integration-test failure that doesn't match the code should check for stray rows from a prior aborted run before assuming a regression.

**Verification, real output:** `db-serial` project alone, 5 consecutive runs, all clean (4 files / 45 tests passing each time, `Prediction` table back to 0 rows after). Full suite (`unit` + `db-serial` together, i.e. what `npm test` actually runs), 5 consecutive runs, all clean (44 files / 625 tests passing each time, ~91-98s each). A 6th full run with `--coverage` also clean (44/625 passing, 98.52% line / 94.63% branch coverage, exit 0). `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint . --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on every touched file.

## 2026-07-30 â€” Share-card Horizon Band glyph/compass-label collision resolved: real font metrics, not a threshold extension

**Why:** NOTES.md (2026-07-29) documented the collision and explicitly named the real fix as a `DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§17 re-composition decision, not a bug fix within the existing budget â€” the existing 50px rule-to-plate budget genuinely cannot fit a below-horizon glyph and the compass label band without collision (shrinking `HORIZON_MAX_DROP` enough to clear it, under 5.75px, was already correctly rejected as too shallow to read as "below the rule" at all). Asked explicitly to resolve it properly and verify with the same rigor as the original plate/contrast work â€” real geometry, not estimates.

**The old "-32.3deg" clearance figure was never checked against real font metrics, and the real number is worse.** `apps/api/src/share/font-metrics.ts` only ever read horizontal advance widths (`hmtx`) â€” no vertical metrics existed anywhere in this codebase to check the old comment against. Extended it to also read `OS/2.sCapHeight` and `OS/2.sTypoDescender` (real values, not estimated): read directly from the vendored Martian Mono file (`OS/2` version 4, `sCapHeight = 800`, `sTypoDescender = -200`, `unitsPerEm = 1000` â€” verified independently with a standalone table read before writing any production code, then pinned in `font-metrics.test.ts` against the same real numbers so the fix can't silently drift if the vendored font file is ever swapped). At `SIZE_MICRO` (15px), a compass label's real ink-top is `800/1000 * 15 = 12px` above its baseline â€” using this real number instead of the old comment's unstated assumption, the actual crossover where a Sun glyph starts touching a compass label is **-25.3Â°**, not -32.3Â°. The old figure wasn't wrong out of carelessness â€” it simply predated any vertical-metrics capability in this codebase to check it against.

**Fix: grow the budget, don't shrink the glyph range.** `HORIZON_MAX_DROP` (16px) is unchanged â€” already a deliberate floor per the pre-existing comment, and not the actual source of the problem. Instead, `COMPASS_LABEL_OFFSET` moved from 26 to 39 (compass label baseline 468 â†’ 481), computed so the deepest possible marker (Sun, radius 9 + 1px stroke = 9.5px half-extent, at -90Â° â€” the full theoretical range, not just the mid-winter-London case NOTES.md happened to name) clears the label's real cap-height with a 1px margin: `ceil((442 + 16 + 9.5) + 12 + 1 - 442) = 39`. `BELOW_HORIZON_LABEL_BASELINE` (486 â†’ 497) and everything from the fact plate down (`FACT_PLATE_Y` 492 â†’ 506, `FACT_LABEL_BASELINE` 512 â†’ 526, `FACT_VALUE_BASELINE` 556 â†’ 570, `FOOTER_BASELINE` 594 â†’ 608) shift by the same uniform +14px, borrowing from headroom already unused at the card's bottom edge (footer ink now bottoms out at 612.2px against a 630px canvas â€” checked against Archivo's own real descender, `210/1000 * 20 = 4.2px`, not assumed). Every existing gap below the fact plate (fact-plate-to-footer, etc.) is identical in size to the pre-fix design, just displaced downward â€” a uniform shift, not a redesign, so nothing needed re-deriving except the two new offsets.

**Verified exhaustively, not at a single boundary.** `og-svg.test.ts` gained a new suite sweeping **every 0.1Â° from 0 to -90Â°** (901 steps â€” the entire possible altitude range, not spot checks) with a Sun marker at azimuth 0Â° (exactly on the "N" tick â€” the worst-case horizontal alignment, which also makes the old "~5deg of a tick" caveat moot: the fix eliminates the row-level collision unconditionally, regardless of azimuth), asserting the rendered glyph's real bottom edge (parsed back out of the composed SVG, not assumed from the input parameters) never reaches the compass label's real ink-top at any step. A second test repeats the check for all four marker types (Sun/Moon/planet/ISS) at -90Â° to confirm the Sun is genuinely the structural worst case, not just the one tested. A third pins the original reported case (Sun at -32.3Â°) as an explicit regression check.

**One real implementation bug caught while writing the sweep test, not shipped:** the first version of the test's marker-glyph parser filtered shapes by `halfExtent > 0`, which let the full-bleed background rect and the two instrument plates through â€” they have no `stroke-width` (the real discriminator between a marker glyph and a plate/background rect) but still produce a positive number from `height/2`, and the background rect's missing `x`/`y` attributes should have produced `NaN` positions but the filter never checked for that. Caught immediately by the sweep test itself failing with `NaN` comparisons on the first run, before it was ever treated as passing; fixed by requiring `stroke-width` presence as the actual discriminator.

**Gates, real output:** the new/changed tests â€” `og-svg.test.ts` (52 tests, all passing, including the 901-step sweep) and `font-metrics.test.ts`'s new real-metrics test â€” run clean. Full `apps/api` gates (both fixes together): `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint . --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean; full `vitest run --coverage` clean.




===== FILE: DESIGN_SPEC.md =====

# ASTRANET â€” Interface Design Specification

**Final consolidated version.** Replaces every prior version of this document. This is the single spec a coding agent should build from â€” no separate addendum files, no version-hopping between v1/v2/v3. Where earlier drafts disagreed with each other, this document is the resolution.

---

## Binding constraints â€” read this before anything else

Three things are explicitly locked and must not be reinterpreted, redesigned, or "improved" by anyone building from this document:

1. **Typography is fixed.** Archivo (display/interface), Martian Mono (measurements), Newsreader (explanatory prose). No substitution, no alternative pairing, regardless of anything else in this document.
2. **The Daily Brief's background system is fixed.** The twilight-interpolated sky ramp â€” the interface's color changing continuously with real computed solar altitude â€” is the product's central visual identity (Â§2) and is retained exactly as designed. Nothing in this document touches it.
3. **The Daily Brief gets minimal visual change.** Its composition, structure, and feel are considered correct. Everything applied to it below is either a genuine bug fix (something already broken against this spec) or a deliberately tiny, single-line addition that reuses existing typography and chrome â€” never a restructuring, never a new visual element with its own weight. If a future change to the Daily Brief doesn't fit in one of those two categories, it doesn't belong on this screen.

---

## Part I â€” Creative Direction

### 1. The problem with how space software looks

Every space product converges on the same visual language: black voids, cyan and violet gradients, glowing hexagons, HUD chrome borrowed from film UI. It is science fiction cosplay. It signals _futuristic_ and communicates nothing about the actual sky.

ASTRANET is not science fiction. It is an **observational instrument**. Its subject is not "space" â€” an abstraction â€” but **the sky above one person, tonight**. That distinction drives every decision in this document.

The reference set is therefore not Blade Runner. It is:

- **Planispheres and star wheels** â€” circular, rotating, aligned to a real horizon
- **Observatory logbooks** â€” dated entries, measured values, honest annotations
- **Marine and astronomical instruments** â€” brass calibration marks, engraved scales, precision without ornament
- **Red-light dark adaptation** â€” the actual working condition of anyone using this outdoors at night
- **Nautical almanacs** â€” dense, typeset, trustworthy tables of when things happen

The product should feel _made and calibrated_, not _rendered_. Closer to a Leica or a barometer than to a dashboard.

### 2. The central design thesis

> **The interface is lit by the user's actual sky.**

ASTRANET already computes the Sun's altitude for the user's exact coordinates. That number is not just data for a card â€” it is the **lighting condition of the entire application**.

The full UI palette is derived at runtime from real solar altitude at the user's location:

| Solar altitude | Twilight phase        | Interface state                                     |
| -------------- | ---------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- |
| > 0Â°           | Day                    | Pale, cool, high-key. Reading light.                |
| 0Â° to âˆ’6Â°      | Civil twilight         | Warming. Contrast softens. Horizon glows.           |
| âˆ’6Â° to âˆ’12Â°    | Nautical twilight      | Deep slate. Brass marks emerge.                     |
| âˆ’12Â° to âˆ’18Â°   | Astronomical twilight  | Near-ink. Airglow tint appears.                     |
| < âˆ’18Â°         | Night                  | Full dark. Minimum luminance. Red accents dominate. |

The transition between phases is continuous, not stepped. **This system is retained exactly as-is per the binding constraints above â€” do not alter it while fixing anything else on the Daily Brief.**

### 3. Aesthetic risk, stated plainly

**The accent color is red, not blue.** ASTRANET uses **astronomer's red** â€” the deep, desaturated red of a dark-adaptation headlamp. Authentic to night observation, functional (preserves night vision, drives a real Red Light Mode), and the strongest available differentiator in a category saturated with cool accents.

**No blue, anywhere.** Not a hover state, not a default component color, not a link. If blue appears in a build, it did not come from this document.

---

## Part II â€” Design System

### 4. Color

#### 4.1 Base ramp â€” "Sky"

| Token     | Hex       | Use                                                 |
| --------- | --------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| `sky-100` | `#EEF1F1` | Day surface. Cool paper, not cream.                 |
| `sky-200` | `#D6DCDC` | Day secondary surface, dividers in day mode.        |
| `sky-300` | `#B4BFBF` | Secondary body text on light surfaces â€” the step between a divider tone and a disabled/tertiary tone. |
| `sky-400` | `#8B9898` | Mid-tone. Disabled states, tertiary text on light. |
| `sky-600` | `#3E4A4A` | Nautical twilight surface.                          |
| `sky-800` | `#1C2424` | Astronomical twilight surface.                      |
| `sky-900` | `#111818` | Night surface. The deepest base.                    |
| `sky-950` | `#0A0E0E` | Immersive/3D scene void. `/explore` only.           |

`sky-950` is the only near-black in the system, exclusive to the 3D scene void. The 2D interface never uses pure black.

#### 4.2 Instrument â€” "Brass"

| Token       | Hex       | Use                                          |
| ----------- | --------- | ---------------------------------------------- |
| `brass-300` | `#C9B187` | Tick marks and scale rules on dark surfaces. |
| `brass-500` | `#9A8258` | Secondary data labels, axis numerals.        |
| `brass-700` | `#6B5A3C` | Brass on light surfaces (day mode).          |

#### 4.3 Signal â€” "Ember"

Live state, alerts, and high-attention events only. Never decoration, never borders, never a gradient, never a default hover color (see Â§4.6).

| Token       | Hex       | Use                                  |
| ----------- | --------- | ------------------------------------- |
| `ember-400` | `#E0614C` | Live-pulse indicator, active state. |
| `ember-600` | `#C4362A` | Primary action, alert emphasis.     |
| `ember-800` | `#7E241C` | Red Light Mode base.                |

#### 4.4 Phenomenon colors

| Token       | Hex       | Phenomenon                                                             |
| ----------- | --------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `aurora`    | `#7FA88A` | Aurora. Muted sage-green.                                               |
| `solar`     | `#D9A05B` | Solar activity, CME, flares.                                           |
| `orbital`   | `#A8B4BC` | ISS and satellites. Cool metallic grey.                                |
| `neo`       | `#B08968` | Near-Earth objects. Dusty rock.                                        |
| `meteor`    | `#C9C4D6` | Meteor showers. Pale chalk-lavender â€” a brief cool streak, not a light source. |

Every phenomenon shown anywhere in the product must map to one of these tokens before it ships. No unassigned phenomenon gets a color improvised at build time.

#### 4.5 Red Light Mode

Toggleable in `/settings`, auto-suggested below âˆ’18Â° solar altitude after an idle-then-returned signal. Single-hue red ramp on `sky-950`, no cool tones, no white text. Not a gimmick â€” the difference between an app usable outdoors at night and one that isn't.

#### 4.6 Interaction states â€” brass, not ember

Hover and focus use brass (`brass-300` on dark, `brass-700` on light). Ember is reserved exclusively for genuinely live or urgent things. If every hover state glows red, red stops meaning "live" â€” this is the rule that keeps ember meaningful.

---

### 5. Typography

**Fixed per the binding constraints above â€” see Â§5.2 for the exact three faces. Nothing in this section is open for reconsideration.**

#### 5.1 The typographic rule that defines the system

> **Words are proportional. Measurements are monospaced. Always, everywhere, without exception.**

The seam this creates â€” visible at a glance, before reading a word â€” is the product's honesty made typographic. This applies at every size, including the Daily Brief headline at its largest.

#### 5.2 The three faces

| Role                    | Face                                 | Reasoning                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------------- | -------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Interface & display** | Archivo (variable)                    | A cartographic grotesque â€” neutral enough for dense data, enough weight range for large headlines. Deliberately not a high-contrast display serif.                                                                                                        |
| **Measurement**         | Martian Mono                          | Tabular figures non-negotiable â€” columns of Kp values and pass times must align.                                                                                                                                                                          |
| **Explanation**         | Newsreader (variable, optical size)   | Used only for plain-language explanations and the Learning Moment. Signals _this is written for you to understand_, not _this is data_.                                                                                                                   |

**Small caps, not stretched capitals.** Where the font supports true small-caps (OpenType `smcp`), use it for micro/eyebrow labels instead of uppercasing regular glyphs. Both Archivo and Martian Mono support this.

#### 5.3 Type scale

| Token        | Size     | Line height | Tracking | Use                                      |
| ------------ | -------- | ------------ | -------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `display-xl` | 5.5rem   | 0.95         | âˆ’0.03em  | The Brief headline. Desktop only.        |
| `display-l`  | 3.75rem  | 1.0          | âˆ’0.025em | Section openers, `/share` card headline. |
| `display-m`  | 2.5rem   | 1.05         | âˆ’0.02em  | Card headlines, mobile Brief headline.   |
| `title`      | 1.5rem   | 1.2          | âˆ’0.01em  | Entry titles.                             |
| `body-l`     | 1.125rem | 1.6          | 0        | Explanation prose (serif).               |
| `body`       | 1rem     | 1.55         | 0        | Default interface text.                  |
| `caption`    | 0.875rem | 1.4          | 0.01em   | Secondary labels.                         |
| `micro`      | 0.75rem  | 1.3          | 0.08em   | Eyebrows and mono stamps. Uppercase.      |
| `data-l`     | 2rem     | 1.0          | âˆ’0.01em  | Featured measurement (mono, tabular).    |
| `data`       | 1rem     | 1.4          | 0        | Inline measurement (mono, tabular).      |

**Headline: hard constraint, not a target.** `display-xl` is set tight, left-aligned, 16 words per line maximum, **two lines total on desktop.** If the composed sentence would exceed that, the backend's summary composer shortens it before it reaches the frontend â€” dropping the least essential clause, never a unit or a confidence qualifier. The frontend has no license to wrap a third line, shrink the font, or otherwise absorb an oversized sentence. A five-line headline is a backend bug, not a frontend styling decision.

#### 5.4 Time, freshness, and Kp precision â€” canonical formats

**Time.** Screens tied to the visitor's own location and moment (Daily Brief, Explore, Best Spot, Settings) show local time, 24-hour, no suffix. Screens meant for someone else to read (Share Card, `/accuracy`) show UTC, explicitly suffixed. Never mixed on one screen.

**Freshness labels.** Exactly two phrasings exist anywhere in the product: **`LIVE`** (fast-tier, paired with the live-pulse dot) and **`UPDATED Xm AGO`** (slow-tier, or fast-tier gone stale). No other phrasing ships â€” not "(NOW)", not a bare bullet, not "TARGET:".

**Kp precision.** Discrete index, never a raw continuous decimal. One decimal maximum, third-step notation preferred (`3-`, `3`, `3+`) wherever it maps cleanly.

---

### 6. Spacing & Layout

#### 6.1 Spacing scale

Base unit 4px: `2 Â· 4 Â· 8 Â· 12 Â· 16 Â· 24 Â· 32 Â· 48 Â· 64 Â· 96 Â· 128 Â· 192 Â· 256`.

#### 6.2 The Observation Grid

The core desktop layout is an asymmetric two-track grid â€” reading track (max 68ch) plus a margin track (240px) carrying freshness, confidence, coordinates, and source. This is original to the product's design, not a new structural change â€” it has simply never been built. Completing it now on the Daily Brief is a correction against this spec, not a redesign of the screen.

```
â”Œâ”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”¬â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”
â”‚   READING TRACK (max 68ch)                  â”‚  MARGIN      â”‚
â”‚                                              â”‚  TRACK 240px â”‚
â””â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”´â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”˜
```

**The margin track is sticky** (`position: sticky`) within its own column, independent of the reading track's scroll â€” this is what actually delivers "permanently visible" rather than a second column that scrolls at the same rate as the first.

#### 6.3 Corner radius

| Token         | Value  | Use                                                                                 |
| ------------- | ------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| `radius-none` | 0      | Rules, dividers, the Horizon Band, data plots.                                      |
| `radius-sm`   | 2px    | Input fields, small controls.                                                       |
| `radius-md`   | 6px    | Buttons, toggles.                                                                    |
| `radius-full` | 9999px | Only genuinely circular objects â€” dial controls, sky-position markers.              |

Sharp things are readouts, soft things are controls. The causal-chain links on the Daily Brief are readouts connected by a measurement, not buttons â€” `radius-none`/`radius-sm`, never a rounded pill.

#### 6.4 Elevation

No drop shadows. Depth via: luminance steps, hairline rules (1px `sky-600` at 40%), one deliberate backdrop-blur exception (`/explore` overlay panels only), and a subtle grain/dither texture on dark surfaces (`sky-800`/`sky-900`/`sky-950` only) â€” both a materiality cue and a real fix for banding in the twilight gradient.

#### 6.5 Persistent Horizon Reference

Once the full Horizon Band scrolls out of view on `/`, a condensed strip pins beneath the top nav: compass ticks and object dots only, 32px tall, no scrubber. Un-pins on scroll-to-top or navigation away. "Where do I look?" shouldn't require scrolling back up to re-answer.

---

### 7. Motion

#### 7.1 Principles

Celestial motion is slow, continuous, inexorable â€” no elastic easing anywhere.

| Class      | Duration   | Easing                  | Use                                                |
| ---------- | ---------- | ----------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
| Micro      | 120â€“180ms  | `ease-out`               | Hover, focus, toggle.                              |
| Transition | 320â€“480ms  | `expo.out`               | Panel entry, card reveal, route change.            |
| Cinematic  | 800â€“1600ms | `expo.inOut`             | 3D camera moves, page-load sequence.               |
| Ambient    | 4â€“20s      | `sine.inOut`, looping    | Atmospheric gradients, star twinkle, aurora drift. |

#### 7.2 The Live Pulse

Fast-tier data carries a single `ember-400` dot, 4px, pulsing once per actual poll cycle â€” never a decorative loop.

#### 7.3 The Freshness Rule

A 1px depleting rule sits under every measurement, full-width in `brass-300` at arrival, depleting across the source's refresh interval, turning `ember-600` at 60% numeral opacity when stale.

#### 7.4 Confidence Ticks

```
Lead      â–®â–®â–®â–®â–®â–¯â–¯â–¯
Agreement â–®â–®â–®â–®â–®â–®â–®â–¯
History   â–®â–®â–®â–®â–¯â–¯â–¯â–¯
          â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€â”€
          MODERATE
```

**Never a percentage. Never a gauge. Never a number next to a bar â€” no exceptions, on any screen, including the Share Card.** Bars plus one qualitative verdict word is the entire display, always. Reveal: each bar fills 0â†’value over 400ms `expo.out`, staggered 60ms, once per data arrival â€” never on re-render.

#### 7.5 Page-load sequence

Under 1.5s total: surface resolves to twilight color (0â€“400ms) â†’ Horizon Band draws (300â€“900ms) â†’ headline sets complete, no stagger (600ms) â†’ entries reveal in 60ms stagger (800ms+) â†’ margin-track provenance fades in last (1200ms).

#### 7.6 Reduced motion

Ambient loops stop, live pulse becomes a static dot with timestamp, freshness rules jump in steps, load sequence collapses to one 200ms fade.

#### 7.7 Anticipatory hover

Hovering a Horizon Band marker (not just clicking) previews identity â€” tether and label at reduced opacity â€” before any navigation. Same principle as Explore's cursor gravity: preview before commit.

---

### 8. Iconography & Illustration

#### 8.1 Icons

Engraved, not drawn â€” 1.25px stroke, square terminals, 20px grid. Instrument-panel engraving, not Feather/Lucide's rounded set. Phenomena use real chart symbols (magnitude dots, directional arcs, okta notation), never icons.

**`/explore`'s cursor** is a small engraved crosshair mark, not the system default arrow â€” a one-pixel reminder the scene is an instrument being aimed.

#### 8.2 Illustration policy

Every visual traces to one of three sources: real data drawn (plots, sky charts, arcs), real imagery (NASA GIBS, always attributed), or instrument marks (ticks, scales, rules). Nothing else ships.

#### 8.3 Object label legibility â€” the decluttering rule

Any screen rendering real-position objects with text labels (Horizon Band, Explore, the Share Card's simplified band) follows one rule: **no two labels may render overlapping or interleaved, at any zoom level, on any screen.** Resolution priority: stagger along the tether â†’ merge into a cluster mark at wider zoom â†’ reveal on hover/tap only. No screen ships a state where two labels sit drawn on top of each other.

---

## Part III â€” The Signature Element

### 9. The Horizon Band

Appears on `/`, `/best-spot`, and `/share/:id`, plus its condensed pinned form (Â§6.5). Full-width strip, 180px tall on desktop, no border, no background fill â€” sits directly on the page surface.

- Horizontal axis: true azimuth, N through W by default (240Â° sweep; full 360Â° only at ultrawide). Vertical axis: true altitude, horizon at bottom.
- The horizon rule is the heaviest line in the product â€” 2px, solid.
- Every marker is at its real position, always. Where two collide, Â§8.3 governs the label, never the marker.
- A time scrubber runs beneath, styled as a brass instrument control (tick-marked rail, small circular brass handle) â€” not a generic web slider, not a stock blue-thumb range input.

**Interaction:** hover previews per Â§7.7; click navigates to `/explore` with the camera pre-oriented to the same viewpoint â€” spatially continuous, not a jump cut.

**Degraded state:** a down source's marker is simply absent, with a margin-track note in the canonical `UPDATED Xm AGO` phrasing. The Band itself never fails â€” horizon, compass, and Sun/Moon positions are pure computation.

---

## Part IV â€” Screens

### 10. `/` â€” Daily Brief

**Everything in this section operates under the binding constraints at the top of this document.** The background system is untouched. The layout below is a correction of what the original spec already called for, not a new direction. The additions listed are deliberately small â€” read each one and confirm it's genuinely a single line, tick, or icon before implementing it as anything more.

#### Composition, top to bottom â€” unchanged from the original intent

**Eyebrow strip** (mono, `micro`, true small caps, `brass-500`): `DELHI Â· 28.61Â°N 77.21Â°E Â· WED 22 JUL Â· CIVIL TWILIGHT ENDS 19:42`. Location switcher reads as part of the line, not a bolted-on control. **One addition only:** when a streak of 2+ consecutive nights exists, it's appended to the end of this same line in the same styling â€” `... Â· 4 NIGHTS RUNNING`. Nothing else changes about this row.

No player chrome beyond the icon itself; playing state is a slow pulse on the icon.

**The Horizon Band** â€” unchanged, full reading-track width, pins in condensed form per Â§6.5.

**The Entries** â€” the original five, in the original order, with the original degradation states (structure-with-em-dashes while loading, dimmed-to-50%-with-a-note when unavailable, never hidden):

Never fails. **Two additions, both minimal:** the existing twilight scale gains two more thin brass tick marks for golden hour and blue hour, styled identically to the phase ticks already there â€” no new element, two more ticks on the one that exists. Not a badge, not a graphic â€” three more words in the same mono line.
2. **ISS pass** â€” next visible pass, direction, peak altitude, duration, brightness, arc diagram. **One addition:** a comfort-temperature reading appended as a fourth measurement in the same row as duration/brightness â€” `8Â°C AT PASS TIME`. Same mono styling as the numbers already there.
3. **Space weather** â€” the causal chain (`FLARE` â†’ `CME` â†’ `Kp` â†’ `AURORA`), rendered as sharp brass-ruled panels, never rounded chips. Confidence Ticks per Â§7.4 exactly â€” bars and a word, never a percentage. No additions here.
4. **Near-Earth object** â€” unchanged.
5. **Learning Moment** â€” unchanged, the one place the serif appears at `body-l`.

**Exit points** â€” unchanged: two text links with a brass rule above.

**What this section deliberately does not add:** no new entry type, no new card, no new visual weight anywhere on this page beyond the six additions named above (streak in the eyebrow, audio icon, two twilight ticks, a supermoon parenthetical, one more mono measurement on the ISS entry). If a future request asks for more than this on the Daily Brief specifically, it should be treated as a new decision, not an extension of this spec.

### 11. `/explore` â€” The Explorable Universe

No new features land here in this version â€” everything speculative for this screen (satellite trains, planet conjunctions, eclipse rendering) was deliberately cut from scope. What remains is correction only:

- The object-label decluttering rule (Â§8.3) applies here exactly as it does on the Horizon Band.
- The engraved crosshair cursor (Â§8.1) replaces the system default.

### 12. `/best-spot` â€” Best-Spot-Tonight Finder

Split 40/60 desktop layout unchanged. Map remains the light-pollution data itself â€” a continuous brass-toned luminance field, not a generic pin map with a dark background.

**Real ranking modes.** Aurora, ISS, and Meteor filters all compute a genuine ranking now â€” not just Aurora with the other two doing nothing. Each filter's active-state underline uses that phenomenon's own token (Â§4.4): `aurora`, `orbital` for ISS, `meteor` for Meteor. Selecting a filter changes each site's third data row to that event's specific detail â€” never a fixed row that ignores the selected filter.

**Extended cloud-cover timeline.** The single "12% cloud" reading becomes a small horizontal sparkline spanning the next 6â€“12 hours, same brass-fill-on-track grammar as the existing three-bar scores, with a thin marker for "now." Degrades to today's single-hour reading if extended data is unavailable â€” never a fabricated flat line.

**Honest degradation, unchanged:** an error state and a populated results list are mutually exclusive â€” never both on screen. If cloud data is down, the clarity bar is replaced with a plain note and the header states the ranking is running on darkness and travel only.

Each toggle carries an honest status line beneath it â€” `PUSH ENABLED` or `PUSH BLOCKED â€” CHECK BROWSER SETTINGS` â€” the same honesty principle the rest of the product already applies to data freshness, now applied to permission state.

### 17. `/share/:id` â€” Shareable Sky Card

Single-viewport, no scroll, no footer (its composition rule stays "no scroll on desktop," which a footer would violate).

If a piece is genuinely unavailable, it's blanked per the degradation contract, never replaced with a bare percentage or a missing headline as a shortcut.

**Discoverability.** Proper meta tags and a sitemap entry for these pages â€” no UI change, a correctness item so the pages that already exist are actually findable.

```
ASTRANET                              STATUS Â· ABOUT
```

An unhealthy source renders dimmed in `ember-600` with its last-known timestamp, matching exactly how a degraded Daily Brief entry presents. No graph, no history â€” that omission is what keeps this an honest instrument reading rather than a marketing status page.

Same voice as the Learning Moment throughout â€” a knowledgeable friend explaining something, never documentation. This page succeeds if someone trusts the confidence number more after reading it, not if it looks impressive.

## Part V â€” Responsive Strategy

Five real conditions, not breakpoints applied after the fact.

**Ultrawide (1920px+):** reading track stays capped at 68ch; extra space goes to a fuller Horizon Band sweep (up to full 360Â°) and an ambient twilight gradient bleeding to both edges.

**Desktop (1280â€“1920px):** the reference implementation throughout this document. Full Observation Grid, sticky margin track, all motion active.

**Laptop (1024â€“1280px):** margin track narrows to 180px, `micro` only, sticky behavior retained. `display-xl` steps to 4rem.

**Tablet (768â€“1024px):** margin track collapses inline beneath each measurement â€” the most significant structural change in the system, designed explicitly per entry, not automated. Best-Spot switches to map-primary with a bottom sheet.

**Mobile (below 768px):** single column, 20px margins. Headline drops to `display-m` but stays dominant â€” never body-sized. Horizon Band becomes horizontally scrollable with cardinal snap points. Causal chain rotates vertical. Red Light Mode surfaced prominently in Settings, since mobile is what people actually take outside.

---

## Part VI â€” Quality Floor

Non-negotiable, unannounced in the UI:

- **Fonts** â€” Archivo, Martian Mono, Newsreader, exactly. Any other typeface anywhere is a defect.
- **Contrast** â€” WCAG AA at every twilight phase, tested at interpolated states, not just the five named ones.
- **Focus** â€” visible 2px `brass-300` outline, never removed, never a browser default.
- **The 3D DOM mirror** â€” every diegetic text element and clickable object mirrored for screen readers; `/explore` fully keyboard-navigable.
- **Motion** â€” `prefers-reduced-motion` respected per Â§7.6.
- **Color independence** â€” no information by hue alone; every phenomenon color pairs with shape or label.
- **Loading** â€” structure before values, always; never a full-page spinner or a skeleton shimmer.
- **Label legibility** â€” no two object labels overlap, at any zoom level, on any screen (Â§8.3), verified against real close-conjunction data.
- **No raw percentages** â€” no confidence or score renders as a bare number anywhere, under any condition, including the Share Card.
- **Palette closure** â€” no element renders in a color outside Part II's tokens. Any blue, or any hex not in this document, is a defect, checked by a lint rule, not by eye.
- **Data parity** â€” any derivative of the Daily Brief (the Share Card, its OG image) computes from the same source object as `/` â€” a smaller rendering of the same data, never a separately-fetched, incomplete one.

---

## Part VII â€” Voice

The interface writes like a knowledgeable friend who respects your time, not a system reporting its state.

| Do                                                                                | Don't                                               |
| ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
| _The ISS passes at 21:42, high and bright._                                       | _ISS transit event detected â€” visibility: nominal._ |
| _We can't reach the space weather service right now. Last reading was at 14:20._  | _Error: SWPC endpoint unavailable (503)._            |
| _Cloud data unavailable â€” ranking on darkness and travel only._                   | _Insufficient data. Confidence degraded._            |
| _About as wide as the Eiffel Tower is tall._                                      | _Estimated diameter: 324m._                          |

Sentence case everywhere except true-small-caps mono labels. Measurements always carry units. Never state a forecast without its confidence, always in bars-plus-verdict form. Never apologize â€” errors explain what happened and what to do next.

---

## Appendix â€” Why this version is shaped the way it is

**Why so little changes on the Daily Brief, when almost everything else in the product grew:** it was already working. The additions here â€” a streak count, an audio icon, two more ticks on an existing scale, three more words on an existing line, one more measurement in an existing row â€” were each chosen specifically because none of them ask the page to hold anything new in the visual sense. They speak in the page's existing voice instead of adding a second one.

**Why the fonts and the twilight background are called out as binding constraints instead of just described:** everything else in this document is open to correction when it's wrong. Those two are not â€” they're the two things about ASTRANET that were right from the start, and the risk with a large refinement pass is that "fixing" things nearby drifts into "changing" things that never needed it.

**Why RSS, multi-language, embed codes, PWA installability, satellite trains, conjunctions, eclipses, and comparison mode aren't in this version:** all were real, considered ideas â€” none were cut for being bad ones. They were cut because this version's scope is deliberately the features that reuse data and infrastructure already in place. They remain a clearly separate future list, not abandoned.



===== FILE: FORMULAS.md =====

# ASTRANET â€” Formulas (FROZEN)

**This document is non-negotiable.** Every constant, threshold, and formula here is frozen. Agents implement these **exactly** â€” do not invent alternatives, do not "improve," do not substitute different constants. If something appears wrong, **flag it in `DECISIONS.md`**; do not silently change it. Every engine function must cite the section number it implements in a code comment.

All angles are in **radians** internally unless a formula explicitly states degrees. All engine functions are **pure**: inputs in, result out, `now` injected as a parameter â€” no clock reads, no I/O.

---

## 0. Constants

```
AU        = 1.496e8      km      (astronomical unit)
R_SUN     = 6.957e5      km      (1 AU â‰ˆ 215 R_SUN)
R_EARTH   = 6371         km
C_LIGHT   = 299792       km/s
PC_KM     = 3.0857e13    km      (1 parsec)
PC_LY     = 3.26156      ly      (1 parsec in light-years)
LD        = 384400       km      (lunar distance)

# Geomagnetic dipole North pole (drifts â€” refresh yearly or use IGRF)
GEOMAG_POLE_LAT = 80.7   deg N
GEOMAG_POLE_LON = -72.8  deg  (i.e. 72.8 W)

# Twilight thresholds (Sun elevation)
TWILIGHT_ISS_AURORA = -6   deg   (civil-ish: dark enough for ISS/aurora)
TWILIGHT_STARS      = -18  deg   (astronomical: faint-star realism)
```

---

## 1. Star Position (catalog â†’ 3D)

Distance from parallax (mas):

```
d_pc = 1000 / parallax_mas
```

**Decision â€” bad parallax:** if `parallax_mas <= 0.2` OR negative â†’ **drop the star**, or pin it to a fixed 100 kpc shell. Never let it produce NaN/negative distance.

Cartesian (RA, Dec in radians; d in parsecs):

```
x = d_pc * cos(dec) * cos(ra)
y = d_pc * cos(dec) * sin(ra)
z = d_pc * sin(dec)
```

Light-travel context:

```
years_ago = d_pc * PC_LY
```

---

## 2. Star Render

Brightness from apparent magnitude:

```
m_ref     = 6.0
brightness = 10 ^ (-0.4 * (m - m_ref))       # clamp to [0, 1]
```

Point size:

```
m_limit    = 6.5
k          = 0.35
point_size = base_size * (1 + k * (m_limit - m))
```

Color temperature (Ballesteros; needs Bâˆ’V). Convert Gaia `bp_rp` â†’ Bâˆ’V:

```
B_V = 0.85 * bp_rp

T_kelvin = 4600 * ( 1 / (0.92 * B_V + 1.7)
                  + 1 / (0.92 * B_V + 0.62) )
```

**RGB rendering â€” explicitly NOT part of the frozen engine (resolved
2026-07-15):** Mapping `T_kelvin` to an RGB color is a rendering/visual-
approximation choice, not a physical constant â€” competing curves (Tanner
Helland's piecewise fit, Mitchell Charity's lookup table) disagree at the
Â±5-10% level with no single correct answer. This step lives in `apps/web`
as a rendering-side utility using **Tanner Helland's blackbody-to-RGB
approximation**, not in `packages/shared`. The shared engine's contract
ends at `T_kelvin` â€” do not add an RGB function to `packages/shared`.

---

## 3. Local Sky Dome (Ground Truth Sky Anchor)

Julian day offset:

```
d_UT1 = JD - 2451545.0
```

Local Sidereal Time (degrees; lon_east positive):

```
LST_deg = (280.4606 + 360.9857 * d_UT1 + lon_east) mod 360
```

Hour angle:

```
H = LST - RA            # (in matching units; convert to radians for trig)
```

Altitude / azimuth (Ï† = observer latitude):

```
sin(alt) = sin(dec)*sin(Ï†) + cos(dec)*cos(Ï†)*cos(H)

az = atan2( -cos(dec)*sin(H),
             sin(dec)*cos(Ï†) - cos(dec)*sin(Ï†)*cos(H) )
```

---

## 4. Sun Position

Use **Meeus low-precision** solar position (accuracy â‰¥ 0.01Â°). Outputs the Sun's RA/Dec and, via Â§3, its altitude for the observer.

Twilight decisions (Sun elevation for the observer):

```
ISS / aurora usable   when  sun_alt < TWILIGHT_ISS_AURORA   (-6Â°)
faint-star realism    when  sun_alt < TWILIGHT_STARS        (-18Â°)
```

---

## 5. Satellite / ISS Visible Pass

satellite.js gives ECI position/velocity; derive look-angles (elevation, azimuth, range) for the observer.

**A pass is "visible" only if ALL three hold:**

```
1) elevation >= 10Â°
2) sun_alt_observer < -6Â°                         (observer in darkness, Â§4)
3) satellite is sunlit:
      let  s_hat = unit vector toward Sun
      let  r     = satellite geocentric position vector
      in_shadow = (dot(r, s_hat) < 0)  AND
                  ( | r - dot(r, s_hat) * s_hat | < R_EARTH )
      sunlit    = NOT in_shadow
```

---

## 6. CME Arrival â€” Drag-Based Model (VrÅ¡nak 2013)

Analytic DBM. Inputs:

```
v0    = CME initial speed        (km/s, from DONKI)
w     = ambient solar wind speed (km/s, from SWPC; default 400 if missing)
gamma = 0.5e-7                   (km^-1, drag parameter)
r0    = 21.5 * R_SUN             (km, DONKI inner boundary)
sign  = +1 if v0 > w else -1     (decel if faster than wind, else accel)
```

Position over time:

```
r(t) - r0 = (1/gamma) * sign * ln( 1 + sign * gamma * (v0 - w) * t )
            + w * t
```

Solve `r(t) = 1 AU` for `t` by **bisection** (r is monotonic in t) â†’ arrival time.

**Arrival uncertainty:** propagate Â±error in `v0` through the same solve â†’ produces the Â±Î”t arrival window.

---

## 7. Aurora Visibility

Geomagnetic latitude (dipole approximation; Ï†, lon in radians; pole from Â§0):

```
sin(Î»_m) = sin(Ï†)*sin(Ï†_p) + cos(Ï†)*cos(Ï†_p)*cos(Î¸ - Î¸_p)
```

where `(Ï†_p, Î¸_p)` = geomagnetic pole lat/lon, `(Ï†, Î¸)` = observer lat/lon.

Equatorward auroral-oval boundary (geomagnetic latitude, degrees):

```
Î»_b = 66 - 2 * Kp
```

Horizon-view margin (aurora at ~110 km is visible low on the horizon from further south):

```
margin_deg = 4
```

**User can see aurora if:**

```
|Î»_m| >= Î»_b - margin_deg
```

Strength cue:

```
strength = |Î»_m| - (Î»_b - margin_deg)     # larger = better placed
```

**Kp source:** primary = SWPC 3-day forecast Kp (`Kp_predicted`). The CME-derived Kp (Â§8) is used **only** for the agreement factor, never as the primary.

---

## 8. Causal Engine â€” Confidence

Three factors, **multiplicative**. All produce values in (0, 1].

Lead-time factor (`t_remaining` = hours until predicted arrival):

```
tau     = 24                                  # hours
f_lead  = 0.3 + 0.7 * exp(-t_remaining / tau)
```

Source-agreement factor:

```
sigma   = 2
f_agree = exp( -|Kp_cme - Kp_swpc| / sigma )
```

History factor (Beta posterior mean; `hits`, `trials` from the accuracy loop):

```
f_hist  = (hits + 2) / (trials + 4)
```

Final confidence and bands:

```
C = f_lead * f_agree * f_hist

C > 0.66          -> "high"
0.33 <= C <= 0.66 -> "moderate"
C < 0.33          -> "low"
```

CME-speed â†’ Kp heuristic (used **only** for `Kp_cme` in the agreement factor):

```
Kp_cme = clamp( round( 1.5 + 2.3 * log10(v0 / 400) ), 0, 9 )
```

---

## 9. Accuracy Loop

For each stored prediction, once its `target_time` has elapsed, fetch observed Kp and score:

```
hit = ( |predicted_kp - actual_kp| <= 1 )
```

Rolling `hits` / `trials` feed Â§8's `f_hist`. Start from the Beta prior (the +2 / +4 in `f_hist`), so early predictions get a neutral, honest prior rather than overconfident 0% or 100%.

---

## 10. Near-Earth Objects

Diameter from absolute magnitude H (assumed albedo 0.14):

```
D_km = (1329 / sqrt(0.14)) * 10 ^ (-0.2 * H)
```

Miss distance in lunar distances:

```
miss_LD = miss_distance_km / LD
```

---

## 11. Best-Spot Score

Each factor in [0, 1]; **multiplicative** so any zero kills the site.

```
clarity  = 1 - cloud_fraction                 # Open-Meteo
darkness = (9 - bortle) / 8                    # Bortle 1 (dark) â†’ 1.0, Bortle 9 â†’ 0
travel   = exp( -distance_km / 50 )            # decays with travel distance

score    = clarity * darkness * travel
```

For aurora nights, multiply by the aurora-visibility factor (from Â§7 `strength`, normalized to [0,1]):

```
score_aurora = score * aurora_factor
```

**Aurora-strength normalization (resolved 2026-07-15):**

```
AURORA_STRENGTH_SATURATION_DEG = 20
aurora_factor = clamp(strength_deg / 20, 0, 1)
```

Rationale: 20Â° of margin inside the auroral oval boundary (Â§7)
comfortably represents "deep in the oval, excellent view"; 0Â° is exactly
at the visibility threshold. `aurora_factor` is only meaningful when
`strength_deg >= 0` (i.e., Â§7's `canSeeAurora` is true). **When aurora is
not visible, do not compute `score_aurora` at all** â€” use plain `score`
(clarity Ã— darkness Ã— travel). Passing a clamped-to-0 factor into
`score_aurora` would incorrectly zero out an otherwise-good site; the
aurora factor should only ever multiply in when it's actually relevant
to that night's ranking, not as a universal fourth term.

Rank candidate sites by `score` (or `score_aurora`, on aurora nights);
recommend the maximum.

---

## 12. Moon Position, Phase & Rise/Set

Meeus low-precision lunar position (Jean Meeus, _Astronomical Algorithms_
ch. 47): mean orbital elements at full precision, periodic corrections
truncated to the largest-amplitude terms only â€” the standard "low
precision" truncation of Meeus's full ~60-term series, same low-precision
tier as Â§4's Sun. Spot-checked against JPL DE421 (via an independent
ephemeris, not this codebase's own math) at three test dates: ecliptic
longitude agreed within 0.31Â°, latitude within 0.21Â°, distance within
190 km â€” consistent with this truncation's well-documented accuracy, not a
coefficient transcription error. This is a "tonight's sky" illustrative
position, not an observatory-grade ephemeris.

Time argument (Julian centuries since J2000.0):

```
T = (JD - 2451545.0) / 36525
```

Mean elements (degrees):

```
L' = 218.3164477 + 481267.88123421*T - 0.0015786*T^2 + T^3/538841 - T^4/65194000   # Moon's mean longitude
D  = 297.8501921 + 445267.1114034*T  - 0.0018819*T^2 + T^3/545868  - T^4/113065000  # mean elongation from the Sun
M  = 357.5291092 + 35999.0502909*T   - 0.0001536*T^2 + T^3/24490000                 # Sun's mean anomaly
M' = 134.9633964 + 477198.8675055*T  + 0.0087414*T^2 + T^3/69699   - T^4/14712000   # Moon's mean anomaly
F  = 93.2720950  + 483202.0175233*T  - 0.0036539*T^2 - T^3/3526000 + T^4/863310000  # Moon's argument of latitude
```

Ecliptic longitude correction, degrees (largest-amplitude periodic terms only):

```
dL = 6.289*sin(M') - 1.274*sin(M'-2D) + 0.658*sin(2D) - 0.186*sin(M)
     - 0.059*sin(2M'-2D) - 0.057*sin(M'-2D+M) + 0.053*sin(M'+2D)
     + 0.046*sin(2D-M) + 0.041*sin(M'-M) - 0.035*sin(D) - 0.031*sin(M'+M)
     - 0.015*sin(2F-2D) + 0.011*sin(M'-4D)

lambda_moon = L' + dL          # apparent ecliptic longitude, degrees
```

Ecliptic latitude, degrees:

```
beta_moon = 5.128*sin(F) + 0.281*sin(M'+F) - 0.278*sin(F-M') - 0.173*sin(2D-F)
            + 0.055*sin(2D-M'+F) + 0.046*sin(2D-M'-F) + 0.033*sin(2D+F) + 0.017*sin(2M'+F)
```

Distance, km:

```
Delta_km = 385001 - 20905*cos(M') - 3699*cos(2D-M') - 2956*cos(2D) - 570*cos(2M')
           + 246*cos(2M'-2D) - 205*cos(M-2D) - 171*cos(M'+2D) - 152*cos(M'+M-2D)
```

Equatorial position â€” rotate `(lambda_moon, beta_moon)` by the obliquity of
the ecliptic `eps` (Â§4's `23.439 - 0.00000036*d`). Unlike the Sun (whose
ecliptic latitude is exactly 0 by definition), the Moon's latitude is
non-zero, so the general rotation applies:

```
alpha_moon = atan2( sin(lambda_moon)*cos(eps) - tan(beta_moon)*sin(eps), cos(lambda_moon) )
delta_moon = asin( sin(beta_moon)*cos(eps) + cos(beta_moon)*sin(eps)*sin(lambda_moon) )
```

Run `(alpha_moon, delta_moon)` through Â§3 (`equatorialToHorizontal`) exactly
like the Sun and stars, for altitude/azimuth at any observer/time.

**Illuminated fraction** (Meeus ch. 48's simpler elongation-angle method â€”
valid since the Earth-Sun distance vastly exceeds the Earth-Moon distance,
so the true phase angle and the geocentric elongation are close enough that
this simplification costs well under 0.5% illumination, confirmed in the
spot-check above):

```
cos(psi) = sin(delta_sun)*sin(delta_moon) + cos(delta_sun)*cos(delta_moon)*cos(alpha_moon - alpha_sun)

illuminated_fraction = (1 - cos(psi)) / 2      # [0, 1]
```

**Phase name.** Illuminated fraction alone can't distinguish waxing from
waning at the same percentage (e.g. 50% is both First Quarter and Last
Quarter) â€” the _signed_ position in the cycle is needed:

```
phase_angle_deg = (lambda_moon - lambda_sun) mod 360   # 0=new, 90=first quarter, 180=full, 270=last quarter
```

Eight equal 45Â°-wide bins, each centered on its defining angle (0/45/.../315),
lower bound inclusive / upper bound exclusive, wrapping at 0/360 â€” matches
common usage where "New Moon"/"Full Moon" name a multi-day window around
the exact instant, not just that instant:

```
[337.5, 360) or [0, 22.5)  -> "new"
[22.5, 67.5)               -> "waxingCrescent"
[67.5, 112.5)              -> "firstQuarter"
[112.5, 157.5)             -> "waxingGibbous"
[157.5, 202.5)             -> "full"
[202.5, 247.5)             -> "waningGibbous"
[247.5, 292.5)             -> "lastQuarter"
[292.5, 337.5)             -> "waningCrescent"
```

**Rise/set.** Find the next time the Moon's altitude (via `equatorialToHorizontal`
above) crosses 0Â°, by the same bisection method as Â§6 (monotonic locally
around each crossing) â€” geometric horizon crossing, no atmospheric-refraction
correction, consistent with Â§3's plain geometric altitude used everywhere
else in this doc.

---

## Implementation Notes (binding)

- **Inject `now`.** No engine reads the system clock directly â€” the current time is always a parameter, so tests are deterministic.
- **Units at boundaries.** Convert degreesâ†”radians explicitly at function edges; keep internals in radians.
- **Clamp where stated.** Brightness [0,1], Kp [0,9], factors (0,1]. Never emit out-of-range values.
- **Monotonic solves use bisection** (Â§6), not Newton â€” no derivative fragility, guaranteed convergence on a bracketed monotonic function.
- **Edge cases are required tests** (Phase 2): negative parallax, Kp = 0 and 9, v0 â‰¤ w vs v0 > w, cloud_fraction = 0 and 1, observer at/near the geomagnetic pole, arrival window crossing tests. Â§12 additionally: each of the 8 phase-name bin boundaries (inclusive/exclusive edges), the 0/360Â° phase-angle wraparound, illuminated fraction at/near 0 and 1, and a rise/set search window too short to find a crossing (must report "not found," not throw or loop forever).

# NOTES.md

Parking lot for ideas explicitly **not** in current scope. Purpose: capture them so they stop pulling focus, without letting them into `WORKPLAN.md` uninvited. Nothing here gets built unless it's deliberately promoted into a phase by the human.

---

## Horizon: Deep-Sky & Cosmological Expansion

The original 34-feature vision's Zones 3â€“5 (Stellar Neighborhood, Milky Way, Large-Scale Universe). Deferred because none of it feeds the personalization/return loop that differentiates the core product â€” it's spectacle, not utility, until there's a genuine reason to return to it.

- **Zone 3 â€” Stellar Neighborhood:** confirmed exoplanet systems (e.g., stand on TRAPPIST-1e), spectroscopic "break the light into a barcode" feature, cosmic-time-machine light-delay callouts.
- **Zone 4 â€” Milky Way:** Sagittarius A* approach (EHT imagery), multi-wavelength spectrum toggle, volumetric nebula fly-throughs.
- **Zone 5 â€” Large-Scale Universe:** cosmic-web filament ride, gravitational lensing distortion engine, CMB "Echoes of Creation" sonification, redshift/dark-energy treadmill, the Pale Blue Dot return-to-Earth moment.
- **Fermi Paradox / Drake Equation filter** â€” interactive overlay on the Milky Way.
- **Gravitational Wave Ripple** â€” live LIGO/GWOSC event visualization.

**Promotion condition:** only once the core loop (Brief, Causal Engine, Explore, Best-Spot, Log, Accuracy) is fully live and there's a specific, considered reason to extend outward â€” not because it would look good in a demo.

---

## Cut Features (explicitly rejected, not just deferred)

These were removed for structural reasons, not just prioritization â€” don't reintroduce without a new argument:

- **Constellation Symphony** â€” isolating/illuminating satellite networks as geometric patterns. Spectacle, no personalization value.
- **Apollo Echoes** â€” historical Moon landing sites + archival audio. Interesting but disconnected from the "now" identity (Pillar 5).
- **Fluid Time Machine Scrubber** â€” dragging through past/future solar-system events. Conflicts with Chronological Synchronicity as a default state.
- **Magnetic Shield Stress Test** â€” magnetosphere deformation visual. Redundant with the Auroral Ring's live data story.
- **Orbital Mechanics Nudge Sandbox** _(from earliest draft)_ â€” lets users alter real orbital data. Violates the live-data trust model outright.
- **Exoplanet Transit Demonstrator** _(from earliest draft)_ â€” static pedagogical demo, not a live phenomenon.
- **Goldilocks Zone Visualizer** _(from earliest draft)_ â€” static overlay, no live data, adds nothing over existing educational tools.

---

## Open Ideas (unscored, unscoped â€” just captured)

- Debris Graveyard Reconstructor (time-reverse a collision/ASAT event) â€” could fit the causal-chain identity if ever revisited, currently out of scope.
- Threat Horizon scale-matched-to-your-city NEO visualization â€” currently folded into the plain NEO card (Â§ workplan Phase 6/10); the "hover a scale model over your city" cinematic version is deferred.
- Barycenter Tug-of-War (Sun wobble from Jupiter) â€” nice detail, no personalization value, low priority.
- Possible future: push notifications (native/PWA) for alerts, beyond in-app only â€” infra decision not yet made.

---

## Non-Ideas (considered and rejected outright, don't revisit)

- Second datastore (MongoDB/Redis) â€” see `DECISIONS.md`, only reconsider if a real query pattern demands it.
- Third-party auth provider â€” custom JWT is the committed path.
- Procedural/fictional object generation of any kind â€” violates the core "everything is real and verified" identity permanently, not just for now.

---

## Known Limitations & Deferred Fixes (real defects, deliberately not fixed yet)

Distinct from the sections above: these are not features anyone chose to defer, they are things currently wrong or fragile, recorded so they are not rediscovered from scratch. Nothing here should be built from without the human moving it into `WORKPLAN.md` first.

### Explore scene â€” cardinal marks can overlap markers at grazing camera angles [RESOLVED - Phase 12]

`apps/web/src/components/explore/CardinalMarks.tsx` renders N/E/S/W as billboarded `DiegeticText` at a fixed 3D radius just above the horizon rule. Fixed in Phase 12 via `filterVisibleCardinalMarks` in `lib/explore-interaction.ts`: culls back-facing cardinal direction labels (`dot < 0.05`) and enforces minimum angular separation (`minAngularSeparationRad = 0.35` ~20Â°).

### Share card â€” deep below-horizon marker glyphs can touch a compass label (RESOLVED â€” see `DECISIONS.md`, 2026-07-30)

Fixed by re-composing the Horizon Band's vertical rhythm (`DESIGN_SPEC.md` Â§17): the compass-label offset, the below-horizon label row, and the fact plate (plus everything anchored to it) shift down by a fixed 14px, using real font metrics rather than the previous non-metric-backed estimate. Verified exhaustively (every 0.1Â° from 0 to -90Â°) in `og-svg.test.ts`. No longer a known limitation.

### `apps/api` test suite â€” `f_hist` assertion flaky under parallel runs (RESOLVED â€” see `DECISIONS.md`, 2026-07-30)

Fixed: the four Postgres-integration test files that write to the global `Prediction` table now run in a dedicated, serialized Vitest project (`apps/api/vitest.workspace.ts`), so they can no longer race each other. Verified with 6 consecutive clean full-suite runs. No longer a known limitation.

## 2026-07-16 (Phase 1 ESLint fixes)

- âœ… Done: Fixed all 38 ESLint errors across the 7 Phase 1 API clients â€” by Antigravity
  - **`require-await`** (celestrak, jpl-horizons, n2yo, open-meteo, nasa, swpc test files): removed unnecessary `async` keyword from `vi.fn()` mock callbacks that returned synchronously; replaced `return new Response(...)` with `return Promise.resolve(new Response(...))` and `throw new Error(...)` with `return Promise.reject(new Error(...))` to preserve the resolved/rejected Promise contract.
  - **`no-unused-vars`** (`celestrak.client.ts`, `swpc.client.ts`): `CELESTRAK_FALLBACK` and `SWPC_FALLBACK` were imported and immediately re-exported, making the import itself unused. Removed from the `import` line â€” the `export { X } from './x.types.js'` re-export on each client's last line already covers consumers without a local import.
  - **`no-redundant-type-constituents`** (`swpc.client.test.ts`): `'error' | 'server_error' | unknown` collapsed to `unknown` (any union containing `unknown` is always `unknown`).

## 2026-07-16 (Phase 3 â€” fast-tier poller loop)

- âœ… Done: Per-source failure handling follows `API_SOURCES.md`'s **documented per-source fallback**, not one generic rule, after re-reading both sources' fallback text closely: N2YO's is "position unavailable" (no stale-value guidance) â†’ a failed fetch's own null-position result is written, marked unhealthy, no stale-data preservation. SWPC's is "use last cached value with an aged freshness stamp; if never fetched this session, unavailable" â†’ a **total** failure (all 5 products null) keeps the previous store value when one exists, still marked `healthy: false` so it's never mislabeled as fresh â€” a **partial** failure (some SWPC products null, matching the client's own per-field degradation) is still written fresh and `healthy: true`, since the source itself did respond â€” by Claude Code
- âœ… Done: `Promise.allSettled` around both client calls so one source's outcome can never affect the other's write (degradation contract, `ARCHITECTURE.md` Â§5); both clients are documented to never throw, but the `.allSettled` + a defensive `startFastTierLoop` tick-level `.catch` guard against anything genuinely unexpected without crashing the interval â€” by Claude Code

## 2026-07-22 (Phase 5 â€” verifyAccessToken guard middleware)

## 2026-07-22 (Phase 6 â€” Task 1: Saved Locations)

- âœ… Done: `apps/api/src/routes/locations.ts` â€” `GET /api/locations` (list), `GET /api/locations/:id`, `POST /api/locations` (create), `PATCH /api/locations/:id` (field updates + default-switch), `DELETE /api/locations/:id`, all behind `requireAuth` and scoped to `req.userId`. A user's first-ever location is always auto-defaulted (no `isDefault` field accepted on create at all, so this can't be bypassed). Cross-user access â€” a real id belonging to another user â€” and a nonexistent id both return an identical generic 404, never a 403, so the API can't be used to probe which location ids exist. Switching the default (`PATCH` with `isDefault: true`) and deleting the current default (which promotes the next-oldest remaining location) are both `$transaction`-wrapped so a mid-flight failure can never leave two locations, or zero, marked default â€” by Claude Code
- âœ… Done: `locations.test.ts` â€” 17 tests against the real docker-compose Postgres (no mocked Prisma Client), including named tests for the two explicitly-called-out behaviors: `first_location_is_auto_defaulted` and three `returns_404_not_403_for_another_users_location_on_{get,patch,delete}` cases, plus default-switch atomicity, delete-promotes-default, delete-of-non-default-leaves-default-untouched, delete-of-only-location, and the `isDefault: false` rejection (un-defaulting is only ever implicit). Test users under `@locations-test.invalid`, cleaned up before/after â€” by Claude Code

## 2026-07-22 (Phase 6 â€” Task 2: Personal Sky Log)

- âœ… Done: `apps/api/src/routes/sky-log.ts` â€” `GET /api/sky-log` (list, newest-first, `?limit=` capped 1â€“500), `GET /api/sky-log/:id`, `POST /api/sky-log` (manual entry create), `DELETE /api/sky-log/:id`, all behind `requireAuth` and scoped to `req.userId`, matching `locations.ts`'s cross-user-404-not-403 contract exactly. ## 2026-07-27 (Follow-up â€” `brief.test.ts` DB-float flake fixed, by Claude Code)

- âœ… Done: `apps/api/src/routes/brief.test.ts` â€” the two prediction-persistence assertions on `Prediction.predictedKp`/`confidence` moved from `.toBe()` to `.toBeCloseTo(â€¦, 12)`. They compared a Postgres-round-tripped `Float` against the in-process double, which is not an equality that holds in general.
- âœ… Done: **precision measured, not guessed** â€” a throwaway probe wrote 800 random doubles through Prisma into the real docker-compose Postgres and read them back (400 across `confidence`'s [0,1], 400 across `predictedKp`'s [0,9], plus the exact value from the observed failure). Results: `extra_float_digits = 1` on PG 16.14, so the loss is **16** significant digits, not the 15 first assumed; **~27% of arbitrary doubles fail exact equality**; max absolute error **5.6e-17** (confidence) and **8.9e-16** (predictedKp), i.e. ~2 ULP. Precision 12 (tolerance 5e-13) is >500x the worst measured error rather than fitted to it â€” the strictest passing precisions (15 / 14) would leave only 5-9x margin on an error that is fundamentally relative.
- âœ… Done: checked the neighbouring float assertions rather than fixing only the line that fired â€” `predictedKp` (line 305) carried the identical latent hazard and was fixed in the same pass; `accuracy.test.ts`'s four `actualKp` assertions were deliberately left on `.toBe()` since they compare against small exact integers (4/5/6/7) that round-trip bit-for-bit.
- âœ… Gates green (real runs): `npx vitest run --coverage` (apps/api) exit 0 â€” 38 files, 394 tests; `brief.test.ts` run **8 consecutive times standing alone, 8/8 green** (pre-fix base rate was ~1 failure per 3 runs); `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint apps/api/src --max-warnings=0` exit 0; `prettier --check apps/api/src/routes/brief.test.ts` clean. - âœ… Done: `GET /api/best-spot?lat=&lon=[&event=aurora][&at=ISO]` â€” generates 33 candidate sites, scores each with the **existing** `packages/shared` Â§11 engine, and returns a ranked list with every component broken out. Verified against an independent formula (spherical law of cosines) and published distances _before_ wiring in: NYCâ†’LA 3935.75 km, 1Â° lon at equator 111.1949 km, pole-to-pole = Ï€Â·R. 100% covered, `packages/shared`'s CI gate still 100/100/100/100 across 15 files / 169 tests.

- âœ… Done: `fetchOpenMeteoBatch` â€” one request for all 33 candidates instead of 33. Multi-coordinate behaviour confirmed against the live API first. - âŒ Found + fixed (pre-existing, latent): the Open-Meteo client returned **naive** ISO timestamps ("2026-07-27T00:00") with no zone even with `timezone=UTC`, which `new Date()` reads as _local_ time â€” a silent +5:30 shift on this machine. Pinned to UTC at the client boundary. Latent only because nothing consumed `hourly.time` before now (Open-Meteo isn't in the poller).
- âœ… Done: degradation per ARCHITECTURE.md Â§5 + DESIGN_SPEC.md Â§12 â€” cloud outage drops clarity **out of the product rather than passing 0** (a zero would kill every site under Â§11's multiplicative rule), flips `ranking.basis` to `darkness-travel`, and carries Â§12's reduced-confidence header note. Left unimplemented deliberately and the comment corrected: kerning is essentially always negative, so the parser over-measures, which wraps early and keeps the CTA inside its margin. An under-measure would clip the card. Tests now assert the true properties, including the one-directional bound.
- ðŸ“ Characterised (not a bug, now documented + tested): **Prisma serializes JSON floats at 16 significant digits**, so a snapshot's raw floats are shortened _on write_ â€” confirmed against raw SQL, and JSONB itself is exact. Harmless here precisely because of `build-snapshot.ts`'s existing decision to **store** `surfaceHex` rather than recompute it from `twilightValue`: every rendered value is a pre-formatted string. Also proved the rounding cannot push a value past the schema's one exclusive bound (`sunAzimuthDeg < 360`) and turn a card permanently `corrupt`.

## 2026-07-29 (Phase 11 â€” correction to the contrast-gap entry above, by Claude Code)

- âŒ **The residual-gap bullet above understated the defect.** It was measured on `ink` alone. Measured per element role _and_ size: ink never fails its applicable floor (headline 75px and fact values 40px are both large text, floor 3:1, worst 3.99 â€” passing everywhere), while **`brass` (15px micro â€” eyebrow, fact labels, compass ticks, marker labels) bottoms out at 1.49:1 and fails on 28.2% of the ramp**, and **the 20px muted CTA bottoms out at 1.52:1 and fails on 44.0%**. Roughly a third of all twilights, not the 3.4% band recorded earlier.
- ðŸ“ **The remedy is now determined rather than a choice of three.** Worst-case achievable contrast for any two-token strategy is `sqrt(contrast(lightToken, darkToken))`: ink 3.979, brass 1.792, muted 1.756, against the 20.25 pair contrast a 4.5:1 guarantee needs. Brass and muted are hue tokens whose variants sit ~3:1 apart, so no threshold, no selection rule and no Â§4.1 ramp narrowing can lift them near 4.5:1.
- âœ… **Recommendation logged in DECISIONS.md: a backing plate behind the instrument text â€” amends Â§17's composition, leaves Â§4.1 untouched.** With existing tokens, a `sky-900` plate gives brass-300 8.67:1, muted sky-400 6.03:1, ink 15.83:1. Ramp narrowing is ruled out (would need ~44% of the ramp deleted); a new ink token is ruled out (needs pure white _and_ pure black, which Â§4.1 forbids).
- âš ï¸ Note for whoever picks this up: `og-svg.test.ts`'s contrast assertions cover `ink`, so they did **not** and do not encode the brass/muted breach. They still guard against ink regressions, but must be extended when the plate lands.

## 2026-07-29 (Phase 12 â€” outage pass + rate-limit budget, code-side, by Claude Code)

- âœ… **Outage/resilience pass done by failure injection at the transport boundary**, not at a seam. `routes/outage.test.ts` stubs the real `fetch`, then drives the real clients â†’ real poller tiers â†’ real store â†’ real Express route, asserting the HTTP response a browser would receive. Every source (N2YO, SWPC, DONKI, NeoWs, JPL Horizons, CelesTrak, Open-Meteo) Ã— five modes (5xx, 503, 4xx, malformed 200, network abort) = 50 tests.
- âœ… **Isolation holds everywhere.** No source failure cascades; the Brief always renders on Sky Anchor; Best-Spot degrades to `darkness-travel`; per-source stale-value policies behave exactly as `API_SOURCES.md` documents (SWPC/DONKI/Horizons/CelesTrak preserve last-known-good marked unhealthy, N2YO/NeoWs do not); recovery on the next healthy tick is clean.
- âŒ **Found: the degradation contract does not hold for space weather.** `build-brief.ts` gates the card on `solarWind.data !== null`, but the poller's total-failure path writes a **non-null object with null fields**, so the gate never opens and a complete SWPC outage yields `spaceWeather.status: 'ok'`. ARCHITECTURE.md Â§5 says the `healthy` flag should drive degradation â€” it is never consulted at card level. The existing unit test passes only because it sets `data: null`, a state the real poller cannot produce. User-visible: `BriefPage.tsx:326` renders `<LivePulse active>` for a dead source, and the dimming, the "unavailable" notice and the Horizon Band's aurora note are all suppressed, while the card body reads "space weather unavailable". **Logged, not fixed** (Phase 12 adds nothing); pinned as two `KNOWN GAP:` tests.
- âŒ **Found: N2YO's real limit for the endpoint that scales with users is 100/hr, not 1000/hr.** Limits are per endpoint ("transaction limited by type"): `positions` 1000, **`visualpasses` 100**. `visualpasses` is observer-specific so it cannot be centrally polled â€” it is called live and **uncached** on every `GET /api/brief` and `POST /api/share`. Practical ceiling â‰ˆ **100 Brief page-loads/hour (~1.7/min)**, worsened by `/` and `/explore` each spending one, and by 3Ã— retry amplification on 5xx (~33 page views worst case). `API_SOURCES.md`'s "upstream load is constant regardless of user count" premise is false for this source.
- âœ… Corrected `API_SOURCES.md`'s rate limit, budget table and binding rule â€” a factually wrong limit would misinform the next capacity decision. Call counts in the new table were **measured** by stubbing `fetch` and running both real tiers: N2YO positions 80/hr, SWPC 178/hr, NASA 18/hr, Horizons 36/hr, CelesTrak 6/hr. All polled sources have comfortable headroom; only the per-request path does not.
- ðŸ“ Noted, not a defect: `fetchWithRetry` is duplicated across six clients but is behaviourally identical (10s timeout, 3 attempts, 500ms doubling backoff, 4xx not retried) â€” confirmed by the outage suite across all five modes. Still six places to miss on a future change.
- â›” Mitigation deliberately **not** built: `cache/store.ts` is a Postgres-backed TTL cache, already swept on a timer, whose header says nothing consumes it yet. Visual passes fit it well, but wiring it is new work and belongs to a phase that owns caching/rate-limit policy.
- âœ… Gates (real runs): `tsc --build --force` exit 0; `eslint .` exit 0; `prettier --check` clean on touched files; `vitest run --coverage` (apps/api) exit 0 â€” **59 files, 817 tests** (up from 767), `All files` 96.99% lines / 93.03% branch.

## 2026-07-30 (Major scope change â€” authentication removed entirely, by Claude Code)

# ASTRA-NET
### Adaptive Sky Telemetry and Realtime Analysis Network
